| `LoadSheetFromFile`'s checks | **identical**: a file that places itself as a parent of itself is refused ("itself as a parent", with the loop named), symbols with no library link (a legacy `R` for `Device:R`) are refused, the references the design already holds are said (an info line: "are numbered anew"), a library the project does not have is a warning (the symbols are drawn from the definitions the file carries), and the reader's own remarks (symbols without a definition were left out). Nothing is written by a check (`POST /api/sch/sheet_file`) | `SCH_EDIT_FRAME::LoadSheetFromFile`; `Board::check_sheet_file`, `eda_kicad::load_sheet_file` |
| Frame and library symbols | a screen read from KiCad places a symbol by its library origin, this design by the corner of its box: the screens are converted when they come in, so what the exporter writes back is what kicad-cli reads. The file's library symbol is used when the design's has the same pins; a name whose pins differ comes in under `_N` and the symbols that used it follow | `Board::add_sheet_tree`, `merge_lib_symbols` |
| Draw Sheet from File (the action with a file) | the parameter is the path (or `{ path, keepAnnotations }`), the file is checked at once ("File ... does not exist." is a toast) and the tool is armed to draw the sheet; with no parameter the chooser opens with Place as sheet forced | `actions/schSheetFileActions.ts`; `DrawSheet`'s `wxFileExists` |
| Drag and drop of a file (`DdAppendFile`) | missing: the window takes drops now (section 20) and says so, but Import Sheet reads a schematic by its path on the server and a dropped file has none (its sheets and libraries are found beside it) | `ui-parity-missing.json` |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/sch_hierarchy_tests.rs` (11: Replace All over the sheets, a pasted sheet, cut and paste, the file as a sheet, as contents, numbering by the Annotate options, Keep annotations, the checks, names counting up),
`crates/cli/src/sch_hierarchy_tests.rs` (the server: the file is read into the command, undo is one step, a file that is not there or not a schematic changes nothing, a copied sheet pasted with its screens, the annotate report, the file list and the checks;
kicad-cli's ERC after Import Sheet, after pasting the sheet and after Annotate over the whole schematic leaves no `unannotated`, `duplicate_reference` or `duplicate_sheet_names` finding and, besides, only the `pin_to_pin` of two power flags
on one net -- the file has its own `PWR_FLAG` and so does the design -- `EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`), `crates/kicad/src/sheet_file.rs` (the reader: a tree, a missing child, a file that is not a schematic: 3), `kicad-port/schImportSheet.test.ts` (8), `kicad-port/schClipboard.test.ts` (the sheet in the paste preview).
Verified in the browser pane on a scratch board made from `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml` with a `.kicad_sch` of `examples/ldo.yaml` beside it: Place > Import Sheet... listed the file, the chooser read it ("6 symbols, 4 libraries (...)", "6 reference(s) of the sheet are used in
this schematic already (C1, C2, D1, J1, R1, U1) and are numbered anew."), Open armed the tool, two clicks opened the properties (name `ldo_stage`, file `ldo_stage.kicad_sch`), Import added the sheet numbered by the page (C501...U501) with 17 wires inside; Ctrl+D on the new
sheet carried a ghost of it and a click dropped `ldo_stage1` with the file `ldo_stage1.kicad_sch` and its own C503...U502, selected; Import with Place as sheet off and one click put the six symbols on the root numbered by the sheet number (C101...U101).

Limits, recorded rather than hidden: the sheet a file places is always a copy (no shared screen); a reference in an imported file that is not annotated (`R?`) is numbered unless Keep annotations is on, in which case it comes in as `R?1`, `R?2`...; "Place as group"
is not offered; a power flag in the file and one in the design on the same net are two flags on one net, which ERC reports as `pin_to_pin`, as KiCad does.

## 19b. Text variables in schematic texts, fields and the title block (`${VAR}`; GAPS.md item 3, P9a; `SCH_FIELD::GetShownText`, `SCH_TEXT::GetShownText`, `SCH_SYMBOL::ResolveTextVar`, `SCH_SHEET::ResolveTextVar`, `SCHEMATIC::ResolveTextVar`, `TITLE_BLOCK::TextVarResolver`, `PROJECT::TextVarResolver`)

The project's variables are one list (Board Setup > Text Variables, `set_text_variables`; the same list as the board's, KiCad keeps them in the project file). `${NAME}` in a free text, a symbol's fields
(Reference, Value, Footprint, Datasheet and the user fields) and the sheet's title block is expanded by `eda_model::text_vars` and the state carries the stored text with a `shown` form beside it where a variable changes it
(`GET /api/schematic`: `texts[].shown`, `symbols[].fields[].shown` or `shown` for a sheet read from a KiCad file, `title_block.shown`), which the painter draws; the editors and the Properties panel keep showing the stored text.
Built-ins: `REFERENCE`, `VALUE`, `FOOTPRINT`, `DATASHEET`, `FOOTPRINT_NAME`, `FOOTPRINT_LIBRARY`, `NET_NAME(n)`, `NET_CLASS(n)` (the effective class's name), `#` and `##` (the sheet's page and the sheet count), `SHEETPATH`, `SHEETNAME`, `FILENAME`, `FILEPATH`,
`PROJECTNAME`, `CURRENT_DATE` and the clock (the browser's, sent with the request), the title block's `TITLE`, `ISSUE_DATE`, `REVISION`, `COMPANY`, `COMMENT1..9`, `${R1:Value}` cross references to another symbol's field, and the project's own variables. kicad-cli's plot of a sheet shows the
same: proved in the slow tier (`kicad_clis_schematic_plot_expands_text_variables_as_the_studio_shows_them`: a sheet with variables plots as a sheet with the studio's shown text written out). Not done: labels, text boxes and the fields of power symbols (they are drawn as stored), `@{}` math,
`VCSHASH`, and a field of a symbol inside a sheet child is resolved from the sheet in view.

## 20. Import Graphics (`sch_drawing_tools.cpp::ImportGraphics`, `dialog_import_gfx_sch.cpp`, `graphics_importer_sch.cpp`; GAPS.md item 28)

File > Import > Graphics... (`eeschema.EditorControl.importGraphics`, Ctrl+Shift+F) and a DXF or SVG dropped on the sheet (`eeschema.EditorControl.ddImportGraphics`) bring a drawing onto the sheet in view. The files are read by the readers of PARITY-pcb.md section 29
(`crates/kicad/src/{dxf_import,svg_import}.rs`); `Cmd::ImportSchGraphics` (`crates/ops/src/import_graphics_sch.rs`) makes the items, one undo step in the schematic's own history, and is no connectivity edit (a drawn line is not a wire).

| Item | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| The dialog: File: with Browse... (or a dropped file), Placement ("Interactive placement", or "At" X, Y), Import scale, Default line width, Default units; no layer, group or discontinuities (`DIALOG_IMPORT_GFX_SCH`). The studio shows the same description of the file as the board's dialog and has the same typed-origin-over-the-scale arithmetic | **done** (`components/ImportGraphicsDialog.tsx` `SchImportGraphics`, `kicad-port/importGraphics.ts` `buildSchImportCmd`; the line width starts at 0, the sheet's default, as the dialog's empty field gives) | `dialog_import_gfx_sch.cpp`, `dialog_import_gfx_sch_base.cpp` |
| A line becomes a graphic line (`SchLine`) with its width (no width is the sheet's default, `MapLineWidth`), its dash style and colour (in the stroke table, `SCH_LINE::SetStroke`); a zero-length line is skipped | **done** | `GRAPHICS_IMPORTER_SCH::AddLine`, `MapStrokeParams` |
| A circle, an arc (three points by rotating the start, a radius over INT_MAX/2 a segment), a polygon (filled with the outline's colour, or with the shape's own: `FILLED_SHAPE` or `FILLED_WITH_COLOR`) and a curve (a Bezier shape; one that is a segment is a line) are shapes (`SchGraphic`) | **done** | `AddCircle`, `AddArc`, `AddPolygon`, `AddSpline` |
| A shape with no stroke (an SVG shape that is only filled: `-1`, "historically no stroke in Eeschema") | **approximated**: a 1 micrometre hairline, the IR cannot say "no outline" | `MapStrokeParams` |
| Text becomes a text item (`SchematicText`): position, size = the file's height, an angle along a row or up a column (the nearer of the two); the file's justification moves the anchor (by the text's width, and for a top or middle alignment its height) to the left end of the baseline and is not kept | **approximated** | `AddText` |
| Interactive placement: the drawing's origin goes to the cursor's grid point (`delta = cursorPos`), the new items are selected and carried by the Move tool: the command runs with `carry: "begin"` (the step is held open, `crates/cli/src/carry.rs`, which holds a schematic step as it holds a board one), a click drops them (the drop joins the step: the import and where it was dropped are one undo step of the schematic), Escape leaves nothing behind; "At" puts the origin at X, Y over the scale | **done**, **differs** in the preview as on the board (`placeNewSchItems` and `commitSchHeld` in `state/store.tsx`: the items are on the sheet while carried) | `SCH_DRAWING_TOOLS::ImportGraphics` |
| A DXF or SVG dropped on the Schematic tab opens the dialog with the file; a `.kicad_sch` dropped there is said to be beyond a drop (see section 19), an image has no item to become | **done** | `m_acceptedExts` of `sch_edit_frame.cpp` |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/import_graphics_sch_tests.rs` (13, with the symbol's), `crates/cli/src/board.rs` (the import is one undo step of the schematic and of the Symbol Editor, each in its own history), `kicad-port/importGraphics.test.ts`. Browser: `e2e/files-in.check.js` (PARITY-pcb.md section 29) on the Schematic tab: the entry in File > Import, the dialog without a layer, a DXF at 10, 5 mm (four lines and four arc shapes spanning x 10 to 50 mm, y -25 to 5), the sheet's default width, one undo, scale 2 with a 0.15 mm default width becoming 0.3,
an SVG's polygon of its own stroke width, interactive placement with the drawing's origin on the cursor's grid point and the Move tool carrying 8 items, Escape leaving nothing behind, a click dropping them with the import as one undo step, and the refusals.

Left: a text keeps no justification and turns to the nearer of 0 and 90 degrees; the schematic image drop and the Import Sheet drop (section 19).

## 21. Pins as items; Swap Pins, Swap Pin Labels, Swap Unit Labels (`sch_selection_tool.cpp`, `sch_pin.cpp`, `sch_edit_tool.cpp::SwapPins / SwapPinLabels / SwapUnitLabels`, `sch_tool_utils.cpp`; GAPS.md items 1, 12, 15)

Added 2026-10-12 (`task-sch-details`). A pin of a placed symbol is an item of the sheet, as it is in KiCad (`SCH_PIN_T`): it is named `pin:<reference>:<number>` (`#<unit>` after the reference for any unit but the first; `eda_model::ir::pin_item_id`), in the order it
was picked, and every verb that takes pins reads those ids. The pure logic is `kicad-port/schPins.ts` (hit test, picking; 8 tests) and `components/schematic/schPick.ts` (one pick for a click, a right click, a double click and a hotkey); the swaps are `crates/ops/src/sch_pins.rs`
(three `SchEdit` verbs, 13 tests) and the studio's side of them `actions/schPinActions.ts`.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Hover, click and the highlight of a pin | **identical in effect**: the pin's box is `PIN_LAYOUT_CACHE::GetPinBoundingBox` (the line, the name and the number) grown by the accuracy (a quarter of the pin symbol size, 25 mil, at least); an exact hit on a pin wins over the symbol, the fields and everything else under the point; with nothing else there the nearest pin within the pick radius is the last resort. A pin is no movable item: M, G and a drag do not pick it up (`PinPick`: a pin is in the pick only for a click and for the tools that ask for pins) | `SCH_PIN::HitTest`, `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL::SelectPoint`, `GuessSelectionCandidates` |
| Box selection, the filter, the summary | **identical**: a box takes pins only when nothing else is in it; the selection filter has its Pins check; the summary and the hook (`__eda.state().selection`: `{id: "pin:R1:1", kind: "pin"}`) name a pin; Properties of a lone pin opens its symbol's | `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL::SelectMultiple`, `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Properties` |
| Net highlight and Select Node from a pin | **identical**: Highlight Net with the cursor on a pin's end highlights its net, found by `GetNode`'s accuracy steps (it used a 400 px radius); Select Node on a pin's end selects the pin (an exact hit on a pin wins over the wire there) and on the wire the wire | `SCH_EDITOR_CONTROL::HighlightNet`, `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL::SelectNode` |
| Swap Pins | **identical in effect**: needs Preferences > Allow unconstrained pin swaps (checked first, nothing is said, as `if( !allow_unconstrained_pin_swaps ) return 0`) and two or more pins of one symbol; the pins trade places in selection order (1 with 2, then 2 with 3: a cycle). KiCad swaps the pins' geometry in the symbol's own copy of its library pins; the IR keeps that as `SchExtras::pin_swaps` (`{symbol key: {pin: the pin whose place it took}}`, empty when every pin is home), read by every pin position (`eda_engine::placed::pin_points_in`, the net tracer, the move tools) and baked by the writer into a cached copy of the symbol (`lib_name`, `<symbol>_<n>`). The wires stay where they are, so the pins are on each other's nets; the junctions the moved pins need are added and the sheet is cleaned up (`TrimOverLappingWires`, `AddJunctionsIfNeeded`, `CleanUp`). Swapping the same pair again puts them back and leaves no entry; a rename, Annotate and Rename Symbol carry the swap with the reference and a delete drops it. Refused, with its reason: fewer than two pins; pins of two symbols; a symbol of a sheet placed more than once ("Pin swaps are disabled for symbols used by multiple sheet instances") | `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::SwapPins`, `SwapPinGeometry`, `SymbolHasSheetInstances` |
| Swap Pin Labels | **identical**: the net labels on the pins trade their text in selection order; each pin needs exactly one net label (a local, global or hierarchical one) on its connection and no other pin there ("Each selected pin must have exactly one attached net label and no other pin connections.") | `SwapPinLabels`, `findSingleNetLabelForPin` |
| Swap Unit Labels | **identical**: the units of one multi-unit reference trade the labels on their pins, each unit's labels ordered by pin position (x, then y), the texts passed one unit along; refused for pins without exactly one label and for more than one reference | `SwapUnitLabels`, `GetSameSymbolMultiUnitSelection` |
| Menus | the context menu of a selection of pins offers Swap Pin Labels, Swap Unit Labels (more than one unit of the reference is placed) and, with the preference on, Swap Pins (`kicad-port/schContextMenu.ts`); one undo step each, the nets read again after it | `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL`'s menu conditions |
| Not done | | Autoplace Fields does not read the swapped places; Reorganize into Module Sheets draws the symbols with the library's pins from the nets the swap left; a symbol of a sheet placed twice cannot be swapped (KiCad refuses it too). (Pin Helpers, and the swaps through a file's `lib_name` copy and the clipboard, are section 30.) |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/sch_pins_tests.rs` (14: the places trade and the wires stay, a swap and its undo leave no entry, a rename and a delete, a cycle of three, selection order, the junction a swap needs, the refusals, the label swaps and their refusals, the unit swap), `crates/cli/src/board.rs` (`swap_pins_and_labels_are_one_undo_step_and_the_nets_follow`),
`crates/kicad/tests/sch_details.rs` (the writer's cached copy; slow, `EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`: kicad-cli loads the file and reaches the swapped pins where the wires are), `schPins.test.ts`.

## 22. Sheet pins one by one (`sch_sheet_pin.cpp`, `sch_selection_tool.cpp`, `dialog_sheet_pin_properties.cpp`, `sch_edit_tool.cpp::Swap`; GAPS.md item 1)

Added 2026-10-12. A pin of a hierarchical sheet has an id of its own (`shpin_...`), so it is picked, boxed, moved along its sheet's border, edited, deleted and swapped one at a time, and the nets of the hierarchy follow (the sheet pin and the hierarchical label of its name are one net: `eda_engine::hier`). The pure
parts are `kicad-port/schSheetPinItems.ts` (the box and the hit: 7 tests); the dialog is `components/SheetPinPropertiesDialog.tsx`.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Pick, window and crossing box, the Pins filter, the summary | **identical**: the pin's box is the body box of a hierarchical label (the flag and the name from the pin's point into the sheet), grown by the accuracy; an exact hit within a pen's width is taken before a near one | `SCH_SHEET_PIN::HitTest`, `SCH_HIERLABEL::GetBodyBoundingBox` |
| Move (M, G, a drag) | **identical**: the pin slides along the border it is on and the part of the move that leaves the border is dropped; pushed past the end of the border it stops at the corner and one dragged across goes to the nearest border (the text takes its spin from the side: `SetSide`); a wire on the pin goes with a drag and the net stays | `SCH_SHEET_PIN::ConstrainOnEdge`, `SCH_MOVE_TOOL` |
| Sheet Pin Properties (E, a double-click) | **partial**: the name (a combo of the hierarchical labels the sheet's own file has; choosing one takes its shape) and the shape, one `edit_sheet_pin` (one undo step, nothing sent when nothing changed; the pin keeps its id so it stays selected); the text's size, bold and italic (section 31); not the pin's font face and colour (the IR has no place for them) | `DIALOG_SHEET_PIN_PROPERTIES` |
| Delete | **identical**: the one pin goes (a second Delete is refused) and the net through it is cut | `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::DoDelete` |
| Swap (Alt+S) | **identical**: two pins of one sheet trade places and with them the nets they reach; a pin with another kind of item, or pins of two sheets, are refused | `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Swap` |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/sch_sheet_pin_tests.rs` (8: the hierarchy ties the pin to the label of its name, a lone pin moves along its border and the net lets go, the corner and the edge switch, a drag takes the wire, delete cuts the net, a rename ties it to the new label, two pins trade places, a pin swaps only within its sheet),
`crates/cli/src/board.rs` (`every_sheet_pin_verb_is_one_undo_step`), `schSheetPinItems.test.ts`, `schProperties.test.ts`.

## 23. Labels and text: auto-rotate on placement and a text's justification (`sch_edit_frame.cpp::AutoRotateItem`, `sch_screen.cpp::GetLabelOrientationForPoint`, `symb_transforms_utils.cpp::GetPinSpinStyle`, `sch_text.cpp`, `font.cpp::getLinePositions`; GAPS.md items 1, 12)

Added 2026-10-12. Two verbs of `SchEdit` carry what the dialogs now hold: `place_label` and `place_text` (one undo step each; before, a label was a `Cmd::AddLabel` and a text an `AddSchText`, and neither could carry its look), and `edit_label` / `edit_text` got `auto_rotate` and the justification. The flag is `LabelLook::auto_rotate`
(a global or hierarchical label only: `AutoRotateOnPlacementSupported`; KiCad does not write it to the file, so it is not read back) and the justification `SchExtras::text_justify` (`SchTextJustify {h, v}`, an entry only when it differs from left and bottom, which is what a text has always been drawn with; KiCad's file default is centre and centre,
so the writer says `(justify left bottom)` explicitly and the reader reads it back). The ports are `crates/ops/src/sch_labels.rs` (26 tests), `kicad-port/schAutoRotate.ts` (the studio's copy, for the dialog's preview: 10 tests) and `kicad-port/schTextItem.ts` (the text as drawn and boxed: 6 tests).

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Auto on Place Label | **identical**: a global or hierarchical label with Auto takes the spin of what is at its anchor. Wires and graphic lines count as horizontal up to 45 degrees and as vertical beyond; a label at the end of a wire reads on along it and at the other end the other way; in the middle of a wire it gets what the C++ gives it (the last of the overlapping items wins); a pin asks for a spin away from its symbol in every orientation (`GetPinSpinStyle`) and wins over a wire that ends on it (symbols come after lines in `KICAD_T` order); a power symbol asks as its pin does; a bus entry sends the label to the side it is on; a label nothing overlaps keeps its spin | `SCH_EDIT_FRAME::AutoRotateItem`, `SCH_SCREEN::GetLabelOrientationForPoint`, `GetPinSpinStyle` |
| Auto on Label Properties, Move and Drag | **identical**: Label Properties with Auto on rotates the label and ignores the orientation choice; a moved label, and a label dragged along its wire, takes the spin of what it lands on again (`finalize` of the drag); with Auto off the spin picked is kept; Auto on a plain label is refused ("a plain label cannot rotate itself") | `DIALOG_LABEL_PROPERTIES::TransferDataFromWindow`, `SCH_MOVE_TOOL` |
| The dialogs | **identical**: Label Properties and the label tools have the spin and the Auto check (the orientation is disabled while Auto is on and the dialog shows the spin Auto will give); Text Properties and the text tool have the horizontal and vertical justification; the tools remember the last spin, Auto and justification (`m_lastTextOrientation`, `m_lastAutoLabelRotateOnPlacement`, `m_lastTextHJustify`); Repeat Last Item repeats a label and a text with their properties; Global Edit's text size edits the text in place (it used to delete and add it) | `SCH_DRAWING_TOOLS::createNewLabel`, `createNewText` |
| A free text is justified | **identical**: drawn and boxed as it is justified about its anchor, in its own axes (`h` left, centre, right; `v` top, centre, bottom), along the sheet or, at 90 degrees, up it; a new text is centred on its anchor (KiCad's last-used justification starts as centre and centre); the angle is kept upright (`KeepUpright`: any angle is made horizontal or vertical); the file's `(justify ...)` is read and written; copy and paste carry it; Delete drops it | `SCH_TEXT`, `EDA_TEXT::GetTextBox`, `EDA_ANGLE::KeepUpright` |
| Rotate and Mirror of a text | **identical**: Rotate counter-clockwise turns a text that runs along the sheet to run up it; turned again it runs along with the other end on its anchor (justification flipped, not upside down); clockwise flips the justification when the text starts to run up; a mirror flips the justification only along the text: X (`MirrorHorizontally`) flips it for a text that runs along the sheet and leaves one that runs up, Y (`MirrorVertically`) does the opposite; in a selection of several items a text turns and mirrors about the selection | `SCH_TEXT::Rotate90`, `MirrorSpinStyle`, `MirrorHorizontally`, `MirrorVertically` |
| Not done | | a label's own fields (netclass, intersheet references) and a sheet pin's text look have no place in the IR; a text variable `${VAR}` is expanded by section 19b: a justified text is laid out, drawn and boxed from its shown text (`SchematicText.shown`, `kicad-port/schTextItem.ts`) |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/sch_labels_tests.rs` (26), `crates/model/src/sch_extras.rs` (the justification overlay: 4, with `kicad_geom::keep_upright_is_vertical`), `crates/kicad/tests/sch_details.rs` (a justified text is written as KiCad writes it and read back; an angle in a file is made upright; slow: kicad-cli's own writer, `sch upgrade`, writes the justification and the angle back as we wrote them),
`crates/cli/src/board.rs` (`placing_and_editing_labels_and_texts_are_one_undo_step_each`), `schAutoRotate.test.ts`, `schTextItem.test.ts`, `schLastPlaced.test.ts`.

## 24. The net-collision overlay of a drag (`sch_drag_net_collision.cpp`; GAPS.md item 1)

Added 2026-10-12. `SCH_MOVE_TOOL::Main` runs `SCH_DRAG_NET_COLLISION_MONITOR` for every held selection (M, G, a drag): `Initialize` when the move starts and `Update` after each step. `crates/ops/src/sch_net_collision.rs` is that class; it runs in the same call as the move (`sch_move::run_with_overlay`), so `POST /api/sch/move_preview`
answers the preview and the overlay together (`net_collision: { collisions, disconnections }`), and `components/schematic/netCollisionPainter.ts` draws it.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Collision circles | **identical**: a circle at each junction the move would need (`JUNCTION_HELPERS::PreviewJunctions`) between items of different nets when one of them is held; its radius is 1.5 times the preview junction (36 mil) | `analyzeJunction` |
| Disconnection marks | **identical**: each connection the selection had that no longer meets is marked by a circle at each of the two points and the line between them (a symbol moved off its wires: a mark at each pin it left; a drag, which stretches the wires, leaves none) | `collectDisconnectedMarkers` |
| Nets | the net of an item is the one it had when the move started; here the nets are the sheet's own geometry (`eda_engine::nets`: points that share a place, a wire's points and every point on it, labels of one name, power symbols of one net), which is what the connection graph's subgraphs are: wires already joined elsewhere, and nets of one name, are one net, so joining them is no collision; two wire ends that meet make no junction and so no mark, as in KiCad | `SCH_DRAG_NET_COLLISION_MONITOR::Initialize`, `recordItemNet` |
| Drawing | **identical**: the theme's drag-collision colour (`LAYER_DRAG_NET_COLLISION`, red): the fill a third of its alpha, the stroke all of it, a 4 px line (`drag_net_collision_width`); the cursor turns to the warning cursor (`not-allowed`) while there is anything to show | `Update`, `AdjustCursor` |
| Not done | | nothing known; the overlay is for Move and Drag, as in KiCad (a turn or a mirror leaves none) |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/sch_net_collision_tests.rs` (9: a wire dragged onto another net, the same move short of it, nets of one name, wires joined elsewhere, a drag with the wires it stretches, two wire ends, a connection pulled apart, a symbol moved off its wires, a turn), `crates/cli/src/board.rs` (`the_move_preview_carries_the_net_collision_overlay`), `schNetCollision.test.ts`.

## 25. Symbol Fields Table: attribute columns, filters and built-in views (`dialog_symbol_fields_table.cpp`, `fields_data_model.cpp`, `bom_settings.cpp`; GAPS.md item 1)

Added 2026-10-12, over section 7. `crates/ops/src/fields_table.rs` (6 new tests), `components/SymbolFieldsTableDialog.tsx`, `kicad-port/fieldsTableStage.ts`.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Attribute columns | **identical**: `${DNP}`, `${EXCLUDE_FROM_BOM}`, `${EXCLUDE_FROM_BOARD}` and `${EXCLUDE_FROM_SIM}` are columns that read the symbol's flags (`SymbolInstance::{dnp, exclude_from_bom, exclude_from_board, exclude_from_sim}`) as 1 and 0 and write them on every unit of the reference; in the grid they are checkbox cells that stage 1 or 0 (a group whose symbols differ shows an indeterminate box and a click sets them all); Apply is one undo step; "Group By" an attribute splits a group | `FIELDS_EDITOR_GRID_DATA_MODEL::isAttribute`, `getAttributeValue`, `setAttributeValue` |
| Include 'DNP' symbols, Include 'Exclude from BOM' symbols | **identical**: the first (on by default) leaves the Do not populate symbols out of the table and of an export; the second lists the symbols excluded from the BOM (never in an export: `PreviewRefresh` turns it off before `Export`). The filters read the flags the sheet has, not the staged edits, as KiCad's read the symbol itself, so a row just ticked does not vanish from under the cursor | `BOM_PRESET::excludeDNP`, `includeExcludedFromBOM`, `RebuildRows` |
| Built-in views (the Preset box) | **identical**: Default Editing, Grouped By Value, Grouped By Value and Footprint and Attributes, with KiCad's columns, grouping, sort and filters; applying a preset adds columns but never removes one (a column the preset does not show stays, hidden: "BOM Presets can add, but not remove, columns"); the dialog's own view is Default Editing, so an export from it carries DNP, Exclude from BOM and Exclude from Board after the value, as 1 or 0 (`getAttributeValue`); the export of the grouped views goes through kicad-cli's BOM job with `--exclude-dnp` when the filter is on | `BOM_PRESET::BuiltInPresets`, `ApplyBomPreset` |
| Not done | | variants (the IR has none), a sheet's own DNP flag (`ResolveDNP` reads it; the IR has none) and the scope selector |

Tests: `fields_table.rs` (the attribute columns show the flags as 1 and 0, a Group By on an attribute splits a group, Exclude DNP reads the sheet not the staged edit, a symbol excluded from the BOM is listed only when asked and never exported, Apply sets an attribute on every unit, the built-in presets), `crates/cli/src/board.rs` (`the_fields_table_shows_and_edits_the_attribute_columns_and_filters_dnp`), `fieldsTableStage.test.ts`.

Added 2026-10-12. The pure parts are `kicad-port/viewControls.ts` (`dragActionOf`, `dragZoomView`, the auto-pan border and step); the handlers are in `components/SchematicView.tsx`.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Pan, zoom or nothing on a drag | **identical for the middle and right buttons**: Preferences > Mouse and Touchpad has "Middle button drag" and "Right button drag" (Pan, Zoom or None; `input.drag_middle`, `input.drag_right`, both Pan by default); a Pan drag moves the view with the pointer; a Zoom drag scales the view about the press point by `exp( -dy * zoomSpeed * 0.001 )` of the pointer's travel since the last motion (up zooms in) | `WX_VIEW_CONTROLS::onButton`, `onMotion` (`DRAG_PANNING`, `DRAG_ZOOMING`) |

| Auto-pan | **identical for the schematic**: with Preferences > Automatically pan while moving object on, the view pans at the edge of the canvas (a border of 2 percent of the smaller side, at least 2 px; the speed grows with `autoPanAcceleration`, 5 by default) while a wire, bus, line, shape or sheet is being drawn, while items are held by M, G or a drag, while a paste is carried and while a box is dragged; the held preview follows the pointer after each step; the pan stops when the pointer leaves the border | `WX_VIEW_CONTROLS::handleAutoPanning`, `onTimer`, `refreshMouse` |

Browser check: `e2e/sch-details.check.js` (nine scenarios on a served copy of `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml`, 72 checks: pins in the selection, the net highlight and Select Node from a pin, Swap Pins with and without the preference and Swap Pin Labels on U1's PA0 and PA1, a sheet pin moved, edited and deleted, a global label's Auto when placed, edited and moved, a text's justification under Rotate and Mirror, the net-collision overlay of a held wire and of R1, the Symbol Fields Table's attribute columns and DNP filter and the Attributes view,
a power symbol's Properties and Copy as Text, the drag settings and the auto-pan; every scenario undoes itself and the run ends by comparing the sheets and the preferences with how they began). It was run on 2026-10-12 on port 8823: all 11 entries pass. kicad-cli proof: `crates/kicad/tests/sch_details.rs` (`EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`).

## 28. Electrical Rules Checker: exclusions that kicad-cli honours, Delete Marker, Delete All Markers, a click on a marker, Inspect and Fix, Save..., Edit connection grid spacing (GAPS.md item 11, package P15; 2026-10-14)

Ported from `eeschema/dialogs/dialog_erc.cpp` (`OnRunERCClick`, `OnDeleteOneClick`, `OnDeleteAllClick`, `OnERCItemRClick`, `OnSaveReport`), `common/rc_item.cpp`, `eeschema/erc/erc_settings.cpp` (`SHEETLIST_ERC_ITEMS_PROVIDER::DeleteItem`),
`eeschema/sch_marker.cpp` (`SerializeToString`, `DeserializeFromString`), `eeschema/schematic.cpp` (`RecordERCExclusions`, `ResolveERCExclusions`), `eeschema/tools/sch_inspection_tool.cpp` (`InspectERCErrorMenuText`, `CrossProbe`),
`eeschema/tools/sch_edit_tool.cpp` (`FixERCErrorMenuText`, `FixERCError`, `Properties` on a marker) and `eeschema/dialogs/panel_setup_formatting.cpp`. The pure rules are `kicad-port/rcItems.ts`, what a click or an entry does is
`actions/checkerOps.ts`, the dialog `ErcDialog.tsx`, the sheet `SchematicView.tsx`, the writer `crates/kicad/src/erc_exclusions.rs`; section 4 has the rest of the dialog.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| An exclusion reaches kicad-cli, with its comment | **identical for the checks whose marker is known to the letter** | the derived project's `erc.erc_exclusions` holds `kiapi.schematic.ErcExclusion` records -- the kind of finding (`ERCET_*`), the marker's position in nanometres, the uuids of its items, the sheet paths its marker sets (`sheetSpecificPath`, `mainItemSheetPath`, `auxItemSheetPath`) and the comment -- which is what kicad-cli 10.99.0-5334 keeps in a project and matches to the markers of a run (`SCHEMATIC::ResolveERCExclusions`). Its report names the items and where they are and the sheet it lists the violation under, and `eda_kicad::erc_exclusions::marker` builds the rest from the table of what each check's marker sets (read from `erc.cpp` and `connection_graph.cpp`, probed against kicad-cli); the finding carries that marker (`ErcViolation.marker`) and `Cmd::AddErcExclusion` stores it beside the `(check, location)` key (`design.schematic.erc_exclusions[].marker`, additive). The pipe-delimited text (`SCH_MARKER::SerializeToString`) is what kicad-cli reads out of an `erc` section at schema 0 only, with the position in hundreds of nanometres, so none is written; the section says it is at schema 1 (`erc.meta.version`) |
| ... a record that matches nothing | handled | kicad-cli turns it into an excluded marker of its own and lists it beside the real violation; the studio's report leaves that one out (`ErcReport::listed`), and a finding waived in the studio whose marker is not known is shown dimmed with a note ("Waived in this report only") |
| Exclude with comment..., Edit exclusion comment... | **identical**, undoable | `OnERCItemRClick` (`ID_ADD_EXCLUSION_WITH_COMMENT`, `ID_EDIT_EXCLUSION_COMMENT`): the comment is under the row and in the project; it comes back in kicad-cli's report |
| Delete Marker | **identical** | the marker leaves the list and the sheet, the row that followed it is selected (`SHEETLIST_ERC_ITEMS_PROVIDER::DeleteItem( aDeep )`). An excluded marker takes its exclusion with it: `SCHEMATIC::RecordERCExclusions`, which `OnRunERCClick` calls before every run and the project save calls again, keeps the exclusions of the markers that are left (`delete_erc_exclusion`, one undo step). The finding is found again by the next run and is not excluded |
| Delete All Markers | **identical** | with exclusions in the report the question "Delete exclusions too?" (Errors and Warnings Only / Errors, Warnings and Exclusions / Cancel); with the exclusions `Cmd::ClearErcExclusions` forgets them all in one step |
| A left click on a marker on the sheet | **identical in spirit** | `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL` takes an `SCH_MARKER` like any item and `SCH_INSPECTION_TOOL::CrossProbe` selects its row in the dialog when that is up; the marker is the selection, a pin the pointer is exactly on wins. A double click: `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Properties` on a marker is `CrossProbe`, the dialog comes up on its row |
| Inspect entries | **identical** | `InspectERCErrorMenuText`: Show Bus Syntax Help (bus_to_net_conflict), Compare Symbol with Library (lib_symbol_mismatch) |
| Fix entries | **identical but one** | `FixERCErrorMenuText`: Edit Symbol Properties... (simulation_model_issue, footprint_filter, footprint_link_issues), Manage Symbol Libraries... (lib_symbol_issues), Update Symbol... (lib_symbol_mismatch), Annotate Schematic... (unannotated, duplicate_reference); the symbol is selected first when the action works on the selection. "Edit Netclasses..." (undefined_netclass) is left out: Schematic Setup has no Net Classes page |
| Save... (Report file (*.rpt), JSON file (*.json)) | **identical, by running KiCad's own** | `OnSaveReport` over `ERC_REPORT::WriteTextReport` / `WriteJsonReport`: `POST /api/erc/report` runs `kicad-cli sch erc --format report|json --units <the editor's> --severity-*` for the Show boxes on the derived schematic and project; the text goes to the browser as `ERC.rpt` / `ERC.json`, as a download. A fresh run: a marker deleted from the list is in it |
| Edit connection grid spacing... (the menu of an `endpoint_off_grid` finding) | **identical** | `OnERCItemRClick` (`ID_EDIT_CONNECTION_GRID`): Schematic Setup opens on General > Formatting |
| Schematic Setup > General > Formatting: Connection grid spacing | **the one setting of the page the model keeps** | `PANEL_SETUP_FORMATTING`: `SCHEMATIC_SETTINGS::m_ConnectionGridSize`, in mils from `MIN_CONNECTION_GRID_MILS` (25) to 10000, validated as KiCad does; `Cmd::SetConnectionGrid` (one undo step, `design.schematic.extras.connection_grid_mils`, additive, 50 -- KiCad's default -- stored as none), written to the derived project as `schematic.connection_grid_size`, so kicad-cli's `endpoint_off_grid` judges by it. The page's other fields (text sizes, line widths, junction and hop-over sizes, intersheet references, operating-point display) have nothing in the model and are not shown as dead fields |

Not done: the checks whose marker the studio does not know to the letter (`endpoint_off_grid` and `unconnected_wire_endpoint` mark each end of a wire and the report names the wire once; `generic-*` and `unresolved_variable`; `field_name_whitespace`, whose marker names a field by a `kiapi.schematic.ErcSymbolChildReference`; `label_multiple_wires`, `four_way_junction`, `no_connect_connected`, `hier_label_mismatch`, `similar_*`, `same_local_global_*`, `bus_definition_conflict`, `duplicate_pins`) are waived in the studio's report alone; "Edit Netclasses..."; a saved report is a download, not a file dialog.

Tests: `rcItems.test.ts`, `crates/kicad/src/erc_exclusions.rs` (the marker of each known check, the paths, the records), `crates/ops/src/review_tests.rs` (`AddErcExclusion` with a comment and a marker, `ClearErcExclusions`, `SetConnectionGrid`), `crates/kicad-engine` (a finding kicad-cli matched is excluded with its comment, the markers an unmatched exclusion made are not findings, the marker an entry offers, a saved report's format, units and severities),
`crates/cli/src/review_tests.rs`. kicad-cli proofs, slow tier, on kicad-cli 10.99.0-5334 (`crates/cli/src/review_tests.rs`): an ERC exclusion with a comment written by the studio makes kicad-cli's ERC report that finding as excluded with the comment (and lists no marker of its
own beside it), a deleted marker stays deleted -- the exclusion goes and the next runs report a violation again, no excluded marker -- and Undo brings it back; a record that matches nothing adds no finding to the studio's report; a saved report is the file kicad-cli writes (`ERC.rpt`, `ERC.json`) for the severities the dialog shows.
Verified in the browser (port 8830, a pristine placed copy of `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml`, 2026-10-15): the schematic half of `e2e/review.check.js` -- Delete Marker (the list closes up, a run finds the finding again), a left click on a marker on the sheet, Exclude with comment on a symbol pin of a module sheet (the next run:
kicad-cli itself reports it excluded, `kicad_matched`, with the comment), Delete Marker on it (the exclusion goes), Delete All Markers (Errors and Warnings Only, Errors, Warnings and Exclusions), Schematic Setup > Formatting (the connection grid: 100 mils makes kicad-cli find a wire end off the grid, the menu of that finding ends with Edit connection grid spacing...,
which opens the page showing 100, 50 is KiCad's default and stored as none), Save... (`ERC.rpt`, `ERC.json`). The ERC Inspect and Fix entries are covered by the node tests only: the board has no finding of a check that has one. Seen by eye: Schematic Setup > General > Formatting.

## 29. Groups on the sheet (`sch_group_tool.cpp`, `group_tool.cpp`, `eda_group.cpp`, `sch_group.cpp`, `sch_selection_tool.cpp`, `sch_painter.cpp`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp::saveGroup`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr_parser.cpp::parseGroup`; GAPS.md items 1, 12, 17; P10b)

Added 2026-10-13 (`task-sch-groups`). A sheet has groups as the board has them: a group is a named set of items and of other groups, one tree. The list is `SchExtras::groups` (the board's `Group`: `grp_...`, a name, the ids of the members; additive, empty and not written when there are none), and the list edits
(`make_group`, `add_members`, `remove_members`, `dissolve_groups`, `set_group`, the walk of the tree) are `eda_model::groups::GroupHost`'s, **one implementation the board's drawings and the sheet's extras both run** (`crates/ops/src/pcb_groups.rs` and `sch_groups.rs` only say which ids may be members). A member is what `SCH_ITEM::IsGroupableType` allows:
a symbol (its reference, every unit), a wire or bus (the whole polyline the studio keeps; KiCad has a line per segment), a bus entry, a junction, a no-connect, a label of any kind, a text, a text box, a graphic, an image, a sheet and another group; not a pin, a field or a sheet pin (they go along with what has them; `SCH_GROUP_TOOL::Group` takes them out of the selection).
The pure parts of the studio are `kicad-port/schGroups.ts` (the selection rules, the box and the menu rules; 12 tests), `components/schematic/schGroupBounds.ts` and `schGroupPainter.ts`; the verbs are `SchCmd::{Group, Ungroup, AddToGroup, RemoveFromGroup, EditGroup}` (`crates/ops/src/sch_groups.rs`) and the studio's side of them `actions/schGroupActions.ts`.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Group, Ungroup, Add Items, Remove Items | **identical in effect**: Group makes one group of the selected items and groups (fewer than two is nothing, a pin or a field in the selection is left out, an item already in a group moves to the new one) and selects the new group; Ungroup dissolves every selected group (an inner group stays a group) and selects what was in it; Add Items takes a group and the items to put in it (one group only; a group cannot go into what it holds); Remove Items takes the selected members out of the group they are in and selects them. One undo step each; the refusals change nothing; none of the four has a default hotkey, as in KiCad. A group left with fewer than two members dissolves, on the sheet as on the board (KiCad keeps a group of one until it is ungrouped) | `SCH_GROUP_TOOL::Group`, `GROUP_TOOL::Ungroup / AddToGroup / RemoveFromGroup`, `EDA_GROUP::AddItem / RemoveItem` |
| Group Properties | **identical in effect**: the dialog opens with Properties (E, or the context menu's Properties... for one selected group, `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Properties`); a name and the member list; Remove drops the row, + hides the dialog and asks for a click on the sheet (the click may land on an item of a symbol, a pin or a field: it picks the symbol), Enter and Escape as for any dialog; OK is one `edit_group` (the name and the members at once) | `DIALOG_GROUP_PROPERTIES`, `GROUP_TOOL::pickNewMember` |
| Select, enter, leave | **identical**: a click on a member selects the outermost group above it; a double click on a selected group or Enter Group goes into it and selects its members, a click then picks a member (an inner group is picked as that group); a click on an item that is outside the group or on nothing outside its box leaves it, Escape clears the selection first and leaves the group on the second press, Leave Group leaves it and selects the group. A window takes a group only when it holds the group's whole box, a crossing box takes the group of any member it touches; the summary and the hook (`{id: "grp_...", kind: "group"}`) name a group | `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL::EnterGroup / ExitGroup / filterCollectorForHierarchy / SelectMultiple`, `GROUP_TOOL::EnterGroup` |
| The look | **identical in effect**: a group that is selected (and whose parent group is not) or is the one entered is drawn with a box round everything below it, in the anchor colour, and its name above the box when the name fits the box's width; the members are drawn as always; the paste preview draws the same, the pasted group being selected | `SCH_PAINTER::draw( const SCH_GROUP* )` |
| Move, Drag, Rotate, Mirror | **identical in effect**: a group is the items below it, every one moved as it would be alone; a drag stretches nothing that is attached to the group (a group has no connection points: the wires on the pins of a member stay); a lone group rotates and mirrors about its own centre (rotation about the half-grid point nearest the centre of its box, then the group is put back on its place), several selected items about their common centre, and four turns are the identity | `SCH_GROUP::Move / Rotate / Mirror`, `SCH_MOVE_TOOL`, `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Rotate / Mirror` |
| Lock, Delete | **identical**: Lock and Unlock take a group (a locked group, or one with a locked item below it, is not moved, dragged, turned, mirrored or deleted); Delete takes the group and every member below it, the wires on the pins of a deleted symbol as for any deleted symbol; one Undo brings the tree back; a member deleted alone leaves its group | `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::DoDelete`, `SCH_COMMIT::Push` (`CHT_REMOVE`), `EDA_GROUP` |
| Copy, Cut, Paste, Duplicate | **identical in effect**: a selected group is copied with everything below it, the pasted tree has new ids, the references a paste gives its symbols and its own groups, the pasted top group is selected, and the tree moves as a tree; a member that was not copied is left out of the pasted group (a group left with one dissolves); what is pasted while a group is entered joins it | `SCH_EDITOR_CONTROL::doCopy / Paste / Duplicate`, `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::Format( SCH_SELECTION* )`, `SCH_COMMIT::Push` |
| Made inside a group | **identical**: while a group is entered every item a command makes (a symbol, a wire, a label, a text, a duplicate, a paste) that can be grouped and is in no group joins it; the client sends such a command in `in_group` (`Cmd::InGroup`) and the server assigns the ids first, so a new item is found | `SCH_COMMIT::Push` (`enteredGroup->AddItem`), `SCH_MOVE_TOOL` |
| Menus | **identical**: the context menu has Enter Group above everything for one selected group, Leave Group inside one, and the Grouping submenu (Group Items when two or more are selected, Ungroup Items for a selection with a group in it, Add Items for a group and a free item, Remove Items inside the entered group), each on or off by `GROUP_CONTEXT_MENU::update`; the Edit menu and the hotkeys reach the same actions | `GROUP_CONTEXT_MENU::update`, `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL`'s menu |
| The file | **identical**: every group is written as `(group "name" (uuid ...) [(locked yes)] (members "uuid" ...))` after the items, the members sorted by uuid (a group in a group by the inner group's uuid), an empty group is not written; read back as KiCad reads them (`parseGroup`, `resolveGroups`), a member the file does not have leaves the group, a loop of groups made by hand is cut (`GroupsSanityCheck`); the uuids the writer gives the items are the ones the groups list, so a group survives the round trip with the same members. kicad-cli loads a file with nested groups, its ERC runs and `sch upgrade` writes every group back with every member | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::saveGroup`, `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR_PARSER::parseGroup / resolveGroups`, `EDA_GROUP`, `SCH_SCREEN` |
| Not done | | Reorganize into Module Sheets drops the groups of the sheets it moves items out of; the members that are sheets pair with the pasted sheets by their order in a paste; the centre of a lone group about which it turns counts a text or a label by its anchor and a symbol by its body box without the fields; a group of one is not kept (it dissolves, as on the board); Lock on a group is the group's own entry in `extras.locked` and only Delete reads it client-side (the server reads it for every move); a click on a row of Group Properties does not take the view to the item; the Align and Distribute verbs (`SCH_ALIGN_TOOL`) are not made group-aware |

Tests: `crates/model/src/groups.rs` (7: the tree, the leaves, the loop refusal, the pruning), `crates/ops/src/sch_groups_tests.rs` (23: Group, nesting, an item in one group at a time, Ungroup, Add and Remove Items with the loop refusal, Group Properties, the verbs as JSON, a group's tree under Move, Drag, a turn and a mirror of a lone group, the lock, the delete upkeep, a rename, an item made in an entered group, a group belongs to its sheet, a pasted tree with new ids, moves as a tree, joins an entered group),
`crates/cli/src/board.rs` (`every_schematic_group_verb_is_one_undo_step_and_the_tree_survives_the_edits`), `crates/kicad/tests/sch_groups.rs` (8, one slow: the groups written naming the uuids, none for a sheet without, a locked group, read back with the same members, a missing member, a loop, the copy of a group; **kicad-cli** loads nested groups, runs ERC and `sch upgrade` keeps every group, `EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`), `schGroups.test.ts`.

Browser check: `e2e/sch-groups.check.js` (thirteen scenarios on a served copy of `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml`: Group and Ungroup with their selections and undo steps, nested groups with Enter Group, Leave Group and the two Escapes, the pointer (a click, a double click, a click inside, a click outside, a click on nothing), a window and a crossing box, Move by the pointer, a quarter turn four times and a mirror twice of a lone group, a drag that stretches nothing, Lock, Delete with the tree and its undo,
Copy and Paste and Duplicate of a tree, Add Items and Remove Items and Group Properties with the picker, the context menu's Enter Group and Grouping entries, a duplicate made inside an entered group joining it, and the `(group ...)` forms of the derived `.kicad_sch`; every scenario undoes itself and the run ends by comparing the sheet with how it began). It was run on 2026-10-13 on port 8829: all 14 entries pass.


## 30. A user-defined field's place on the sheet (`sch_field.cpp`, `dialog_symbol_properties.cpp`, `dialog_field_properties.cpp`, `fields_data_model.cpp::ApplyData`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp::saveField`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr_parser.cpp::parseSchField`; GAPS.md items 1, 12; P10b)

Added 2026-10-13 (`task-sch-groups`). A symbol's fields beyond Reference, Value, Footprint and Datasheet (`FIELD_T::USER`; `SchematicSection::user_fields`, part-wide by reference, edited from the Symbol Fields Table) are fields of the sheet like the four: each has a place of its own per placed unit in `SchematicSection::field_layout` (`FieldPlacement::name` is the field's own name), so it is listed, drawn, picked, moved,
turned, mirrored, edited, hidden, autoplaced, written to the `.kicad_sch` and read back from one. `eda_engine::fields::symbol_specs_in` is the one place that adds them after the four (by name, hidden until shown, as `ApplyData` and `OnAddField` make them: `SetVisible( false )`; a name that is a standard one's, whatever the case, is no field of its own), and everything downstream reads the specs: the placement and Autoplace
Fields (`symbol_placements`, `place_fields`), `OwnerCtx` (so `EditField`, the move verbs, `AutoplaceFields`), the writer (`write_page_field`) and the studio's sheet (`owner_fields_json`). The studio's side is `kicad-port/schFieldEdit.ts` (the names, the commands; 3 tests), `components/SymbolPropertiesDialog.tsx` and `components/SchFieldDialog.tsx`.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Listed, drawn, picked | **identical in effect**: the server lists a symbol's user fields after the four (`fld:<reference>:<name>`, the text, where it is, whether it is shown), the painter draws a shown one in the Fields colour, and a click, a window and the selection filter take it as any field | `SCH_FIELD`, `SCH_PAINTER::draw( const SCH_FIELD* )` |
| Added, removed, shown | **identical in effect**: Symbol Properties lists them with a Show check and the text to edit; Add Field starts a row called `Field<N>` (`GetUserFieldName`), hidden; a name a field has, whatever the case, or a generated one is refused; the x beside a row removes the field; one OK is one undo step with the rest of the dialog (`SchCmd::AddUserField`, `RemoveUserField`, `EditField`). The Symbol Fields Table makes a column's field on every symbol, hidden, and a column renamed or removed there keeps or drops the places its fields had | `DIALOG_SYMBOL_PROPERTIES::OnAddField / OnDeleteField`, `FIELDS_EDITOR_GRID_DATA_MODEL::ApplyData` |
| Placed, moved, turned, mirrored | **identical in effect**: shown, a field is autoplaced with the others (`AUTOPLACE_AUTO` while the symbol's fields are autoplaced: one line under the Value, on the symbol's free side); M, G and a drag move it by the offset taken back through the symbol's transform and take the item out of the autoplaced ones; R turns its text a quarter where it stands, X flips its justification, a turn of the symbol carries it round with it (its place is in the symbol's own frame) and Autoplace Fields (O) puts it with the others again, honouring its Allow Autoplace | `SCH_MOVE_TOOL::moveItem`, `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Rotate / Mirror / AutoplaceFields`, `SCH_SYMBOL::AutoplaceFields` |
| Field Properties, Delete | **identical in effect**: E opens Edit <name> Field: the text (empty allowed for a user-defined field, not for a Value), the place, size, bold, italic, orientation, justification, Visible, Show Name and Allow Autoplace, one `edit_field`; Delete hides it and keeps its text | `DIALOG_FIELD_PROPERTIES::UpdateField`, `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::DoDelete` (`SCH_FIELD_T`: "Hide field") |
| The file, written | **identical**: `(property "MPN" "text" (at x y angle) [(show_name yes)] [(do_not_autoplace yes)] (effects (font (size ..) [(bold yes)] [(italic yes)]) [(justify ..)] [(hide yes)]))` after the four, where the sheet keeps the field; a hidden one is hidden. kicad-cli loads a file with nested groups and a shown user field, its ERC runs, `kicad-cli sch export svg` draws the text where we put it (and not the hidden one's), and its own writer (`sch upgrade`) keeps the place and the flag | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::saveField` |
| The file, read | **identical in effect**: `import_kicad_sch` reads every property but Reference, Value, Footprint, Datasheet and Description (whatever the case; `(property private ...)` too; the first of a name) as a user field: the text, and the place in the frame of the symbol (`local_placement_on_page`, the inverse of what the writer does), the size, bold, italic, justification, hide (in the property as KiCad 9 writes it, in the effects, or bare), Show Name and Do Not Autoplace; a file written again puts every field where it was, for every turn and mirror | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR_PARSER::parseSchField` |
| Not done | | A sheet read from a KiCad file keeps its user fields and their places (written back as they were) but lists and draws none of its fields (item 12: it has no places for its standard ones), so they are neither shown nor edited there, and Add Field is not offered; `(property private ...)` loses its flag; Copy, Cut, Paste and Duplicate carry a user field's text, not whether it is shown or where (the pasted fields are autoplaced, as every pasted symbol's are); the Properties panel has no row for a user field and Find treats one as hidden (it lists the standard names only); a user field of a power symbol or a sheet has no place in the IR (`user_fields` is by symbol reference); a new field starts at the Reference's place, KiCad's at the symbol's origin (hidden either way); the derivation of module sheets (`hier/kit.rs`) does not count a shown user field among the texts it keeps clear |

Tests: `crates/engine/src/fields/tests.rs` (3: the specs, the placement with the others, a page position into the frame and back for every turn and mirror), `crates/ops/src/sch_user_fields_tests.rs` (19: hidden until shown, autoplaced clear of the others, move, turn, mirror and a symbol's turn, text and look edits, locked, Autoplace Fields, Add and Remove, a rename and a delete, the Fields Table's rename, removal and addition, a sheet read from a file),
`crates/cli/src/board.rs` (`every_user_field_verb_is_one_undo_step_and_the_studios_sheet_lists_the_field`), `crates/kicad/tests/sch_user_fields.rs` (6: written where the sheet keeps it, turned with the symbol, read back with text, look and place, written again where it was for every turn and mirror, a file made by hand, none), `crates/kicad/tests/sch_groups.rs`
(`kicad_draws_a_placed_user_field_where_we_put_it_in_a_sheet_with_nested_groups`, slow: kicad-cli, `EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`), `schFieldEdit.test.ts`.

Browser check: `e2e/sch-groups.check.js` (four scenarios on a served copy of `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml`: Add Field in Symbol Properties with a refused name, Show, the three texts stacked, the file's `(property ...)` at the place the sheet draws, one Undo; a click, Move, R, X, Delete and a symbol's move on the field, each with the file; Field Properties on the text and the look, an empty text; Symbol Properties' edit and remove of a row and Autoplace Fields). It was run on 2026-10-13 on port 8829: all entries pass.


## 31. Pin Helpers; swapped pins through the file and the clipboard (`sch_edit_tool.cpp::PIN_TRICKS_MENU`, `sch_selection_tool.cpp::Main`, `sch_screen.cpp::Append`, `sch_tool_utils.cpp::SwapPinGeometry`; GAPS.md items 1, 12; P10b)

Added 2026-10-15 (`task-sch-groups`). The submenu a selection of pins and sheet pins gets, and what Swap Pins leaves behind for Copy, Paste, Duplicate and a file. The work is `SchCmd::PinHelper` (`crates/ops/src/sch_pin_helpers.rs`, one undo step) and the studio's side of it, `kicad-port/schPinHelpers.ts` (4 tests) and `actions/schPinHelperActions.ts`; the swaps are `sch_import.rs::pin_swaps_between`,
the clipboard writer's `swapped_copy` (`crates/kicad/src/sch_clipboard.rs`) and the paste's remap (`crates/ops/src/sch_clipboard.rs`).

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| The submenu | **identical**: Pin Helpers is the first entry of the context menu of a selection of one or more pins and sheet pins and of nothing else, with Wire, No Connect, Net Label, Hierarchical Label and Global Label; each acts on every pin and sheet pin of the selection (anything else in it is passed over). They are menu ids in KiCad, not tool actions, so here they are the studio's own `studio.PinHelpers.*`: no hotkey, in no menu bar | `PIN_TRICKS_MENU::update`, `S_C::AllPinsOrSheetPins`, `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Init` |
| Which way a pin runs | **identical**: a symbol's pin by its library orientation turned and mirrored by the symbol (a pin that Swap Pins moved runs the way the pin whose place it has runs); a sheet pin by its side (left is `PIN_RIGHT`, right `PIN_LEFT`, top `PIN_DOWN`, bottom `PIN_UP`) | `pinOrientation` (`RotateAndMirrorPin`), `SCH_SHEET_PIN::GetSide` |
| Wire | **identical in effect**: a stub from the pin's connection point one wire-grid step (the grid of the wire tool, 50 mil by default) out of the symbol along the pin's line, a junction where the pin has a wire already, the sheet cleaned up; the new stubs are selected with only their far ends picked and handed to the Drag tool, a click drops them (the drop joins the step: one undo step for the stubs and the drop) and Escape takes them away again (`commit.Revert()`) | `ID_POPUP_SCH_PIN_TRICKS_WIRE`: `SetFlags( ENDPOINT )`, `RunSynchronousAction( SCH_ACTIONS::drag, &commit )` |
| No Connect | **identical in effect**: a no-connect flag on each pin's connection point, nothing selected afterwards; a pin that has one gets no second (KiCad adds it and the cleanup takes it again: the command that would change nothing is refused, so it leaves no undo step) | `ID_POPUP_SCH_PIN_TRICKS_NO_CONNECT`, `SCHEMATIC::CleanUp` |
| Net, Hierarchical and Global Label | **identical**: a label on each pin's connection point saying the pin's shown name, or `<reference>_<number>` for a pin with none (a sheet pin: its name); it reads away from the pin (`LEFT` for a pin that runs right, `RIGHT` left, `BOTTOM` up, `UP` down); the shape of a hierarchical or global label is the pin's type (bidirectional, input, output, tri-state and unspecified as they are, every other type an input; a sheet pin's shape stands for its type, a passive one being an input); the new labels are the selection | `ID_POPUP_SCH_PIN_TRICKS_{NET,HIER,GLOBAL}_LABEL`, `SetSpinStyle`, `SetShape` |
| A symbol with swapped pins, in a file | **identical in effect**: a symbol's own copy of its library symbol (`(lib_name "<symbol>_<n>")`, which the writer has always written for a symbol Swap Pins moved) is read back as the places traded (`SchExtras::pin_swaps`): each pin of the copy that is where another pin of the library symbol is, the exchange being a permutation; so the wires are on the pins they were on, for every turn and mirror, and a second write gives the same copy. A copy this cannot read so (a pin where no library pin is, or where several are) reads as the library symbol, as it always did | `SCH_SCREEN::Append` ("a new name must be created"), `SwapPinGeometry` |
| ... and on the clipboard | **identical in effect**: Copy writes the symbol's own copy of its library symbol with the places traded and `(lib_name ..)` on the instance (its sub-blocks `<copy>_<unit>_<style>`, which KiCad requires), Paste and Duplicate read it, number the symbol anew and keep the swap under the new reference; kicad-cli reads the clipboard text and derives the nets with the wires on the pins they were on | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::Format( SCH_SELECTION* )`, `SCH_EDITOR_CONTROL::Paste` |
| Not done | | KiCad warps the pointer to the stubs' end when the Drag tool takes them; a page cannot, so the held stubs follow the pointer's travel from where they start; a swap in a file the writer wrote for two instances of one library symbol turned differently is not read (the writer bakes each entry through one instance's turn, so the copy and the library symbol are not in one frame); Autoplace Fields does not read the swapped places |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/sch_pin_helpers_tests.rs` (13: a stub out of each pin along its line, of a turned and mirrored symbol, on the wire grid asked, a junction where a wire is, a no-connect once, the labels' names, spins and shapes by electrical type, a turned pin, a swapped pin, a sheet pin's side, shape and name, the refusals, the JSON), `crates/cli/src/board.rs` (`every_pin_helper_is_one_undo_step`),
`schPinHelpers.test.ts`, `crates/kicad/tests/sch_pin_swaps.rs` (5: a file with swapped pins reads back with the swap for each turn and the nets as drawn, a symbol with its pins reads with none, a second round trip, the clipboard text and its reading, and -- slow, `EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1` -- kicad-cli reading the clipboard with the wires on the pins they were on), `crates/ops/src/sch_clipboard_tests.rs` (a pasted symbol keeps its swap).

Browser check: `e2e/sch-groups.check.js` (five more scenarios: the submenu of a pin's context menu and Net Label with its undo; No Connect on two pins, its refusal, Hierarchical and Global Label; Wire held by the Drag tool, dropped, undone and cancelled with Escape; a sheet pin's global label on the root sheet; Duplicate and Copy and Paste of a symbol with swapped pins). It was run on 2026-10-15 on port 8829: all entries pass.


## 32. A sheet pin's text look (`dialog_sheet_pin_properties.cpp`, `sch_label.cpp::SCH_HIERLABEL::GetSchematicTextOffset`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp::saveSheet`; GAPS.md items 1, 12; P10b)

Added 2026-10-15 (`task-sch-groups`). A hierarchical sheet pin is an `EDA_TEXT` (`SCH_SHEET_PIN` is a `SCH_HIERLABEL`): Sheet Pin Properties sets the size, bold and italic of its name as Label Properties does a label's. The IR keeps them as `SchExtras::sheet_pin_looks` (a `LabelLook` by the pin's id; absent is 50 mil, regular, upright, so a design that never set one serializes as before).

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Sheet Pin Properties | **identical in effect**: Text size (0.01 to 1000 mm: "Don't allow text to disappear"), Bold and Italic beside the name and the shape; OK is one `edit_sheet_pin` (`size_um`, `bold`, `italic` with the name, the shape and the place), one undo step, nothing when nothing changed; the look stays with a pin that is renamed or moved and goes with a pin or a sheet that is deleted | `DIALOG_SHEET_PIN_PROPERTIES::TransferDataFromWindow` |
| Drawn and picked | **identical in effect**: the flag grows with the size and the name is set in it (a bold text with a fifth of its size for a pen, an italic one leaning), a quarter of the text offset plus the size out from the flag, along the sheet on a left or right border and up it on a top or bottom one; the pin's box for a click and a window is that of its size (the default text is 50 mil: the painter drew it 1 mm before, with the name of a top or bottom pin lying) | `SCH_HIERLABEL::GetSchematicTextOffset`, `GetBodyBoundingBox` |
| The file | **identical**: `(pin "NAME" shape (at ..) (uuid ..) (effects (font (size S S) (bold yes) (italic yes)) (justify ..)))`, read back by the pin's uuid | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::saveSheet`, `parseSchSheetPin`, `parseEDA_TEXT` |
| Not done | | the pin's font face and colour; Copy, Paste and Duplicate of a sheet make it with no pins, as they always did, so a look is not carried; a pin made again by Sync Sheet Pins or Autoplace All Sheet Pins is a new pin with the default text |

Tests: `crates/ops/src/sch_sheet_pin_tests.rs` (3: a look set and cleared by the default, the refusals, the look kept by a rename and a move and gone with the pin or the sheet), `crates/kicad/tests/sch_user_fields.rs` (`a_sheet_pins_text_look_is_written_and_read_back`), `schSheetPinItems.test.ts` (the command and the box), and `e2e/sch-groups.check.js` (Sheet Pin Properties with a size that would vanish refused, 2.54 mm bold italic set, the file saying so, one Undo). The whole file (23 scenarios, 152 checks: groups, user fields, Pin Helpers, swapped pins and this) was run on 2026-10-15 on port 8829: all pass.


## 33. Import Non-KiCad Schematic File (`eeschema/files-io.cpp`, `sch_io_altium.cpp`; GAPS.md item 28, package P22; 2026-10-15)

`eeschema.EditorControl.importNonKicadSchematic` was recorded as missing because the studio had no importers for other formats. The installed kicad-cli's `sch import` runs KiCad's `SCH_IO` plugins (Altium, Eagle, CADSTAR, EasyEDA and EasyEDA Pro, LTspice, PADS, DipTrace, P-CAD, OrCAD, EasyPC) and writes a `.kicad_sch` with the library symbols in a `.kicad_sym`;
our `.kicad_sch` reader (`load_sheet_file`, the one Import Sheet uses) turns it into the design. PARITY-pcb.md section 34 is the board half, the engine and the findings.

| Behavior | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| File > Import > Non-KiCad Schematic File... (`eeschema.EditorControl.importNonKicadSchematic`, the Schematic tab): a file chooser (`.sch` of Eagle, `.SchDoc` of Altium, `.csa` of CADSTAR, EasyEDA `.json`, `.zip`, `.epro`, LTspice `.asc`, ...); the page posts the file as base64 (`POST /api/import_foreign`, `kind: "schematic"`), kicad-cli converts it, the server reads the result and answers with its sheets and library symbols (a `SheetFile`) and the counts | **done** (`actions/importActions.ts`; the parameter `{ name, data }` is a file the page already has) | `SCH_EDIT_FRAME::OnImportProject`, `importFile` |
| What it does to the open schematic: **replaces it**, the root sheet and every sheet under it, with the nets traced from the new drawing (against nothing: every net of the new drawing is a net the command made) and the library symbols the old drawing published; the symbols' definitions come in as a paste's do (`merge_lib_symbols`: the design's own definition of a name wins, a different drawing of it comes in under another name). `Cmd::ReplaceSchematic` is **one undo step** of the schematic's history; the board stays (its parts stay, and the symbols of the file are parts it has not placed). The file's frame is KiCad's (`imported_from_kicad`: a symbol sits at its library origin), as for a schematic read by `eda import-kicad` | **done** (`crates/ops/src/import_foreign.rs`; `crates/cli/src/import_foreign.rs`: the nets, the parts, the undo; `tests/studio_import_foreign.rs`: over HTTP, with the mcu30 example drawn as module sheets for the file: more than 40 pins on nets over its three sheets, the undo and the redo) | `SCH_EDIT_FRAME::importFile` (`SetSchematic`, `RecalculateConnections`) |
| What differs from KiCad: it asks first ("This operation replaces the contents of the current schematic, which will be permanently lost. Do you want to proceed?") and clears the undo list (`ClearUndoRedoList`); here nothing is lost for good (Undo brings the old drawing back), so it does not ask and the toast says so. A non-KiCad schematic has no drawing sheet (`SetEmptyLayout`, `empty.kicad_wks`): the studio's page frame is the file's paper | **differs**, said in the toast | `SCH_EDIT_FRAME::OnImportProject`, `importFile` (`DS_DATA_MODEL::SetEmptyLayout`) |
| The reader on what kicad-cli makes: for the QA schematics (`qa/data/pcbnew/plugins/eagle` and `eeschema/io/eagle` `.sch`, the two Altium `.SchDoc` sheets, EasyEDA's `SCH_*.json`) the symbols our reader makes are the symbols the converted file places (counted from its text), which are the symbols kicad-cli's report states (Eagle's Adafruit AHT20: 36; Eagle's test file of two sheets: 24; Altium's `power.SchDoc`: 49 and `it6251_core.SchDoc`: 124; EasyEDA's: 135) | **done**, **slow test** (`import_foreign_qa.rs::kicad_cli_converts_the_qa_schematics_...`) | -- |
| A schematic of several top-level sheets (Eagle's `eagle-import-testfile.sch` has two): the sheets that no sheet places are top-level sheets of a KiCad 10 project, and `kicad-cli sch import` writes one file for each (`x.kicad_sch`, `x_2.kicad_sch`, ...) of which only the first is reachable from the output (it says "2 sheets were written, but only 'x.kicad_sch' is reachable" and writes no project to name the others). Read as it is, 4 of that file's 24 symbols were missing. The route and the CLI read the other files from beside the output and place the sheets on a root as an Altium project's are (`sheet_file_of`, `top_level_siblings` in the order of their numbers, `combine_sheets`), and the notes say so | **done** (`crates/cli/src/import_foreign.rs`: the order of the files and the grid; slow: the oracle sums the symbols of every file, `import_kicad.rs` reads the file with its board) | `SCH_IO_EAGLE::LoadSchematicFile`; `sch import` is `kicad/cli`, which the source checkout does not have |
| The findings: Altium's "No library symbol found for schematic symbol ..." lines (one per symbol the file has no library entry for) and the report's errors ("Could not find image file 'C:\\...': The file path may be specific to a different operating system."), each once with how often it was said, in the Import Issues dialog | **done** | `SCH_IO_ALTIUM`, `LOAD_INFO_REPORTER` |
| An Altium project (`.PrjPcb`): kicad-cli takes one `.SchDoc` at a time. `eda import-kicad Project.PrjPcb` reads the project's `[DocumentN]` / `DocumentPath=` entries, converts the board and every sheet, and places the sheets on a root as `SCH_IO_ALTIUM::LoadSchematicProject` does: a sheet symbol each on a grid of 1000 mil (two apart, ten across), 500 x 150 mil, pages from 2, the hierarchical labels of the top-level sheets turned into global labels, a sheet another sheet places not placed again | **done** in the CLI (`combine_sheets`; the QA project's two sheets come in as `it6251_core` and `power`, pages 2 and 3, 124 + 49 symbols) | `SCH_IO_ALTIUM::LoadSchematicProject` |

Browser check: `e2e/import-foreign.check.js` (PARITY-pcb.md section 34, 53 checks) imports Altium's `power.SchDoc` on the Schematic tab: the action is offered there and refused on the PCB tab, "Import Issues" lists the two missing images, the sheet in view has the file's 49 symbols (30 parts and 19 power symbols), pins are on nets, the board's parts are still there, and one Undo from the Schematic tab brings the old drawing back.

**Approximations, and what is left.**
- An Altium project's variants (`ParseAltiumProjectVariants`: a project can say which components are not fitted or have alternates) are not read: the IR has no variants (GAPS.md, design variants). Only the studio's one-file route is missing the project: a `.PrjPcb` is not a file kicad-cli's `sch import` takes, so the project is the CLI's.
- Library symbols of Altium files that name a library the file does not hold (the "No library symbol found" ones) are left out by the importer, and so are their symbols; the findings say how many.
- LTspice, DipTrace, OrCAD, EasyPC and P-CAD schematics are converted by kicad-cli and read like any other, but none is in the QA data here.

## 34. Design blocks on the schematic (`sch_design_block_control.cpp`, `sch_design_block_utils.cpp`, `design_block_control.cpp`, `design_block_pane.cpp`, `panel_design_block_chooser.cpp`, `sch_drawing_tools.cpp::ImportSheet` and `DrawSheet`, `sch_editor_control.cpp::PlaceLinkedDesignBlock`, `eda_group.cpp`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp::saveGroup`; GAPS.md item 32; P18)

Added 2026-10-20 (`task-design-blocks`). A design block is a `.kicad_blocks` library folder with one `<name>.kicad_block` folder per block (`<name>.kicad_sch`, an optional `<name>.kicad_pcb` and `<name>.json`: description, keywords, default fields), listed by the `design-block-lib-table` beside the symbol and footprint
tables (`PARITY-common.md` section 14 for the tables, `crates/kicad/src/design_block.rs`, `crates/cli/src/design_block_api.rs`). The pane, the dialogs and the actions are `components/designBlocks/*`, `actions/designBlockActions.ts` and the pure rules `kicad-port/designBlocks.ts` (tree, menu, names), `kicad-port/svgPreview.ts` (the preview).
A group keeps the block it was placed from or saved to: `Group.design_block` (`EDA_GROUP::m_designBlockLibId`), written and read as `(lib_id "Lib:Name")` in the `(group ...)` of the `.kicad_sch` and `.kicad_pcb`, and kept by Copy, Paste and Duplicate.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| View > Design Blocks: the pane | **identical in effect**, a docked column on the right of the sheet: search box, the library tree with Recently Used (the last seven blocks) and pinned libraries first, the search of `libChooser.ts` over the nickname, name, `Lib:Name`, keywords and description (`DESIGN_BLOCK::GetSearchTerms`), the selected block's description, keywords and default fields, kicad-cli's drawing of its schematic as the preview (`sch export svg`, cached until the file changes), the boxes Place repeated copies, Place as group (on), Place as sheet and Keep annotations | `DESIGN_BLOCK_PANE`, `PANEL_DESIGN_BLOCK_CHOOSER`, `DESIGN_BLOCK_TREE_MODEL_ADAPTER`, `SCH_DESIGN_BLOCK_PANE` |
| The pane's menu | **identical**: Pin / Unpin Library, New Library..., Place Design Block, Properties..., Save Current Sheet as Design Block..., Save Selection as Design Block... (with a selection), Update Design Block from Current Sheet and from Selection (with a selection), Delete Design Block, Hide Library Tree; each only while its condition holds | `DESIGN_BLOCK_CONTROL::AddContextMenuItems`, `SCH_DESIGN_BLOCK_CONTROL::Init` |
| Place Design Block (Shift+B, a double click on a block) as contents | **identical in effect**, one undo step: `import_sheet` with the block's file and `block`: the items arrive at the click, in a group named after the block and linked to it (Place as group), numbered anew unless Keep annotations (the power symbols are numbered anew either way), the group selected; with Place repeated copies the tool stays armed and the groups are named `Name1`, `Name2`, ... | `SCH_DRAWING_TOOLS::ImportSheet` (`placingDesignBlock`, `placeSheetContents`, `suffix++`) |
| ... as a sheet (Place as sheet; Draw Sheet from Design Block) | **identical in effect**: two corners, then the sheet's dialog with the sheet named after the block and its file `<name>.kicad_sch`; Import copies the file in, numbers its symbols anew, and the sheet goes alone in a group linked to the block | `SCH_DRAWING_TOOLS::DrawSheet` (`drawSheetFromDesignBlock`) |
| Place Linked Design Block / Save to Linked Design Block (a linked group's menu) | **identical**: the block the one selected group is linked to becomes the pane's selection and is placed again, or updated from the group; "Could not find design block %s." and "Design block %s does not have a schematic file." as KiCad says them; the entries are shown for a group and greyed when it has no link | `SCH_EDITOR_CONTROL::PlaceLinkedDesignBlock`, `SaveToLinkedDesignBlock`, `groupWithDesignBlockLink` |
| Save Current Sheet as Design Block... | **identical in effect**: refused without a library ("Please select a library to save the design block to.") and for a sheet with sheets in it ("Design blocks with nested sheets are not supported."); Design Block Properties opens with the sheet's name; a name that exists asks "Overwrite existing design block?"; the block's schematic is the sheet's file (`export_kicad_sch_tree`) | `SCH_EDIT_FRAME::SaveSheetAsDesignBlock`, `checkOverwriteDb` |
| Save Selection as Design Block... | **identical in effect**, the link is one undo step: the selection's items (a lone group stripped to its members; a lone sheet is the sheet's save) are the block's schematic; the dialog opens with the group's name, else the screen's; afterwards the items are in a group named after the block and linked to it, or the lone group takes the link ("Group Items", "Set Group Design Block Link"), and that group is selected | `SCH_EDIT_FRAME::SaveSelectionAsDesignBlock` |
| Update Design Block from Current Sheet / from Selection | **identical in effect**: "Design block '%s' already has a schematic." / "Overwrite existing schematic?"; the sheet's update opens the dialog with the name locked and the block's fields (the sheet's over them); the selection's keeps the metadata | `UpdateDesignBlockFromSheet`, `UpdateDesignBlockFromSelection`, `checkOverwriteDbSchematic` |
| Properties..., Delete Design Block | **identical**: Properties saves the metadata, and under a new name saves the block there and deletes the old one (asking when the new name exists); "Library '%s' is read only." for a library the studio cannot write; Delete asks "Delete design block '%s' in library '%s' from disk?" and says "Design block '%s' deleted from library '%s'" | `DESIGN_BLOCK_PANE::EditDesignBlockProperties`, `DeleteDesignBlockFromLibrary` |
| Group Properties' "Library link:" | **identical**: the box shows the group's link; a text that is no `Lib:Name` is refused with "Invalid library link: '...'" and the dialog stays; empty clears it; `edit_group` carries it, one undo step | `DIALOG_GROUP_PROPERTIES::TransferDataFromWindow` (`LIB_ID::Parse`) |
| The file | **identical**: `(group "Name" (uuid ...) (lib_id "Lib:Name") (members ...))`; kicad-cli reads it and `sch upgrade` keeps the link; a linked group of one item is kept (KiCad keeps it) | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::saveGroup`, `parseGroup` |
| Not done | | a placed sheet does not get the block's default fields as hidden fields, and a saved sheet's default fields start empty (the IR has no user fields on a sheet); a block name with a '.' is refused (KiCad would hide the block, `DesignBlockEnumerate` cuts the name at the first dot); Import Sheet's chooser and the pane keep check boxes of their own (KiCad shares them: `UpdateCheckboxes`); the pinned libraries, recents and check boxes are the browser's (localStorage); the pane is docked, not floating |
