Tests: `crates/ops/src/sch_hierarchy_tests.rs` (4: a block placed as contents is one linked group whose members follow the numbering; a file's contents grouped only when asked and with no link; a block placed as a sheet goes alone in a linked group; the link verb and Group Properties' link), `crates/kicad/tests/sch_groups.rs` (the group's `lib_id` round trip, kicad-cli's `sch upgrade` keeps it),
`crates/model/src/groups.rs` (a library id is `Lib:Name`; a linked singleton is kept), `crates/cli/src/design_block_tests.rs` (11), `kicad-port/designBlocks.test.ts`, `svgPreview.test.ts`, `schImportSheet.test.ts` (`node --test`). The kicad-cli proofs (slow tier, `EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`): the schematic blocks this studio writes (a selection's and a sheet's) load in kicad-cli (`sch export svg`, `sch erc`), and a block made from KiCad's own `Arduino_Nano` template is placed through Import Sheet with its two headers, a linked group and the nets its labels name.
Verified in the browser (port 8836, a pristine copy of `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml`, 2026-10-20): `e2e/design-blocks.check.js` is a 106-step check through `window.__eda` that passes. Its schematic half: the actions on their tab, View > Design Blocks and New Library, Save Current Sheet as Design Block (the dialog's three refusals, the files, the details, kicad-cli's preview, a sheet with sheets refused), Save Selection (the group, the question), Place as contents (grouped, renumbered, one undo step, repeated copies named 1 and 2), Place as sheet, Place Linked, Update (both questions, the locked name), Properties (the rename, the overwrite question), Delete, Group Properties' link, the tree's search, double click and menus.

## 35. Tables on the sheet: Draw Table, Edit Table, the structure edits and the table's Properties (`sch_table.cpp`, `sch_tablecell.cpp`, `sch_edit_table_tool.cpp`, `edit_table_tool_base.h`, `sch_drawing_tools.cpp::DrawTable`, `sch_painter.cpp::draw( SCH_TABLE )`, `sch_selection_tool.cpp::SelectColumns`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp::saveTable`, `parseSchTable`; GAPS.md item 24; P16t)

Added 2026-10-21 (`task-tables`; the board's tables, which share the model, the verbs and the dialogs, are `PARITY-pcb.md` section 38). A sheet's table is a grid of text-box cells like the board's, an IR item of its own (`SchExtras::tables`, the same `eda_model::table::Table` with no layer; an additive overlay), read and written as the `(table ...)` of the `.kicad_sch`
(`crates/kicad/src/sch_table_io.rs`), and sent to the studio with every cell's corners, shown text and placed lines worked out by the server (`crates/cli/src/table_json.rs`, `eda_model::table_text::sch_cell_draw`). Every edit is one undoable verb of `Cmd::SchTable` (`{"op": "sch_table", "verb": ...}`, `crates/ops/src/pcb_tables.rs`: the board's `TableCmd` on the sheet in view); a table is an
item the sheet's own verbs know (`lockable_ids`, groups, `Item::Table` of the move scene). A sheet counts from 0 where a board counts from 1, and draws its text with the sheet's pen; the rest of the table's rules are the board's.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| The file: `(table ...)` with `(cells (table_cell ...))` | **identical**: the column count, the border and separators, the column widths and row heights, the uuid and the lock (`(locked yes)`), and each cell's text, `exclude_from_sim`, `at`, `size`, margins, `span`, fill and `effects` as `saveTable` writes them (a stroke with no width is the default 6 mil, a cell with no `(effects)` is left and top, one with `(effects)` and no `(justify)` is centred); the table of KiCad's QA sheet `BusAndVectors` comes back through our reader and writer and is plotted by kicad-cli as it was; a group names a table by its uuid | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::saveTable`, `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR_PARSER::parseSchTable` |
| A cell's shown text | **identical**: `${ROW}`, `${COL}` and `${ADDR}` count from 0 (`A0`), `${CELL("A0")}` and `${CELL(row, col)}` name another cell's text, then the sheet's variables (`${TITLE}`, its fields, the project's); the text is broken to the column's width and its lines placed by `SCH_TEXTBOX::GetDrawPos` (a standing table swaps the axes and the margins) and `GetLinePositions`, drawn with the sheet's default pen when the cell's is automatic; a table we write is inked within 4 um of where kicad-cli inks it, lying and standing | `SCH_TABLECELL::GetShownText`, `SCH_TEXTBOX::GetDrawPos`, `EDA_TEXT::GetEffectiveTextPenWidth` |
| Place > Draw Table (the right toolbar) | **identical in effect**, one undo step ("Draw Table"): the first click is the first corner, the cursor sizes the grid (as many columns as hold 15 glyph widths of the sheet's 50 mil text, as many rows as hold 2 glyph heights, at least one of each; a cell at least 5 by 2 grid cells, rounded to the grid) and a preview shows it, the second click opens Table Properties, and the table (a border and separators of the default width, margins of 0.953 mm) is on the sheet when OK is clicked; the tool stays armed | `SCH_DRAWING_TOOLS::DrawTable` |
| Table Properties, Table Cell Properties, Edit Table (Ctrl+E) | **identical in effect**: the board's dialogs on the sheet (the grid of texts, the border and separators; the cell's text, font look, size, alignment, margins, Column Width, Row Height), with the note that a sheet counts from 0; no layer. The cell's fill and text colour are not in the dialog (the Properties panel has the fill) | `DIALOG_TABLE_PROPERTIES`, `DIALOG_TABLECELL_PROPERTIES` (eeschema) |
| Add Row Above / Below, Add Column Before / After, Delete Row(s) / Column(s), Merge Cells, Unmerge Cells, Select Column(s) / Row(s) / Table, Export Table to CSV | **identical in effect**, the board's code (`Table::add_row` ..., `kicad-port/tables.ts`) on the sheet's tables; Export Table to CSV writes the shown text | `EDIT_TABLE_TOOL_BASE`, `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL::SelectColumns`, `SCH_EDIT_TABLE_TOOL` |
| A click, a window, the selection filter | **identical in effect**: a click picks the cell under it (`hitTestDistance`), a window takes the table; tables and cells are items of the Text category; a selected table or cell is drawn in the selection colour over a wash of it | `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL::Main`, `SCH_PAINTER::draw( SCH_TABLE )` and `draw( SCH_TEXTBOX )` for a cell |
| The painter | **identical in effect**: each cell's background first (none, the outline's colour, the sheet's background, or a colour of its own), then its lines in the stroke font, then the border and separator lines (`SCH_TABLE::DrawBorders`) with the stroke's colour, width (0 is the default) and dashes; a cell another one covers is not drawn | `SCH_PAINTER::draw( SCH_TEXTBOX )`, `SCH_TABLE::DrawBorders` |
| Move, Drag, Rotate, Mirror, Lock, Group, Delete | **identical in effect**: a selected cell stands for its table in Move and Rotate (`RequestSelection( .., aPromoteCellSelections )`), the move preview shows the held table where it would be (the server's cells and lines); Rotate turns a table between its two attitudes (the text angle goes 0 to 90 and back, `SCH_TABLE::Rotate`) about the half-grid point nearest the centre of its box; Mirror does nothing (`MirrorHorizontally` is empty); Lock locks the table (a cell has no lock of its own); a group of two that loses its table (deleted) dissolves; Delete on a selected cell clears its text and on a table takes it away (`DoDelete`) | `SCH_MOVE_TOOL::Main`, `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Rotate`, `Mirror`, `DoDelete`, `SCH_TABLE::Rotate` |
| The context menu and the Properties panel | **identical in effect**: for a selection of cells Select Column(s), Row(s) and Table, then Add Row/Column, Delete Row(s)/Column(s), Merge Cells and Unmerge Cells (for a block, for a merged cell), Edit Table... and Export Table to CSV; the panel has Start X and Y and the Table Properties group for a table and, for a cell, Text, Italic, Bold, the justifications, Text Size, the four margins, Column Width, Row Height and the Cell Properties group (Background Fill, Background Fill Color) | `SCH_SELECTION_TOOL::Init`, `EDIT_TABLE_TOOL_BASE::addMenus`, `SCH_TABLE_DESC`, `SCH_TABLECELL_DESC` |
| Not done | | Copy, Cut, Paste and Duplicate of a table or of cells (and `pasteCellsIntoSelection`); a drag across cells selecting the block under it; `@{expression}` is shown as typed and a `${REF:FIELD}` is not turned into the symbol's id (`ConvertRefsToKIIDs`), so it does not follow a renamed reference; the text colour, the hyperlink and a font face of a cell are kept by the reader and the writer and edited nowhere; the Table Cell Properties dialog has no fill |

Tests: `crates/kicad/tests/sch_tables.rs` (2: a table written into the `.kicad_sch` and read back with its cells, lock and group; a sheet with no table writes none), `crates/kicad/src/sch_table_io.rs` (5), `crates/model/src/{table_text,table,text_vars}.rs` (the sheet's shown text and its lines), `crates/ops/src/sch_tables_tests.rs` (7: Draw as the sheet's tool makes it, every edit of the board on the sheet, move and the two attitudes, a locked table, grouping and delete, a selected cell standing for its table, the cell's background, the wire format),
`crates/cli/src/table_json.rs` and `table_tests.rs` (the move preview carries a held table where it would be, its text variables resolved), `kicad-port/{tables,schTableProperties,schContextMenu,schSelectionPrune,schFieldEdit,actionTabGate,edaTestHook}.test.ts` (`node --test`).
kicad-cli proofs, slow tier (`EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`, `crates/kicad-engine/tests/tables_sch.rs`): a table of ours on a sheet is plotted by `sch export svg` with each line of text within 4 um of where the same table of KiCad's own writing is plotted, lying and standing; the table of KiCad's QA sheet `BusAndVectors` read and written again is plotted as it was.
Verified in the browser (port 8838, a board made from `examples/mcu_board_30plus.yaml`, 2026-10-21): `e2e/tables.check.js` passes (5 scenarios, 59 checks). Its sheet half: Draw Table through its two clicks and Table Properties, Add Row Above keeping the selected cell, the Properties panel for a cell and for a table, R on a selected cell turning the table to its standing attitude and back, Lock and Unlock, Delete and Undo, and the context menu of a cell (Merge Cells only for two cells side by side). By hand: the preview of Draw Table following the cursor, a cell's text and Background Fill edited in the panel, a drag of a cell moving its table (and Undo giving it back), the standing table with its text turned, Edit Table opening its dialog, and the Copy and Duplicate refusal ("nothing in the selection can be copied").

## 36. The schematic's image: Place Image, the drop of an image file, Image Properties (`sch_drawing_tools.cpp::PlaceImage`, `sch_edit_tool.cpp::DdAddImage`, `dialog_image_properties.cpp`, `panel_image_editor.cpp`, `sch_bitmap.cpp`, `sch_painter.cpp::draw( SCH_BITMAP )`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp::saveBitmap`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr_parser.cpp::parseImage`; GAPS.md item 24, package P16i; 2026-10-21)

Added on `task-pcb-items`. An image on a sheet is `SchGraphicKind::Image { at, scale_ppm, data }` (`crates/model/src/sch_extras.rs`), a graphic like the shapes, so the verbs that take any graphic take it (`add_graphic`, `edit_graphic`, `delete_graphic`, `set_locked`, Move, Rotate, Mirror, Group, Copy and Paste by their text). `data` is the file (PNG, JPEG, GIF or BMP) as the base64 of its `(data ..)`; the state describes an image (`crates/cli/src/image_api.rs`: its place, scale, size in pixels, definition and a hash) and `GET /api/sch_image?id=..&h=..&sheet=..` serves the file, so the page never carries the pixels. The size and the definition are read from the file's header (`eda_model::image_info`, shared with the board's reference images: `PARITY-pcb.md` section 39); no pixel is decoded in Rust.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| An image in a `.kicad_sch` | **real item, was dropped on import**: `(image (at x y) (scale s) (uuid ..) (locked yes) (data "..." ..))` is read with its centre, scale (a scale that is not a positive number is 1.0), lock, group and the file, in a file of version 20230121 or older with the scale times the image's definition over 300 ("older versions assumed 300 PPI"); written as `saveBitmap` writes it (scale one is left out, the data in lines of 76 characters) and read by kicad-cli, whose SVG plot has the image at the place and of the size this studio computes (centred on the point, 6 x 4 mm for 120 x 80 pixels of 254 per inch at half scale; slow tier) | `parseImage`, `saveBitmap`, `SCH_BITMAP::Plot` |
| Place Image (`eeschema.InteractiveDrawing.placeImage`) | **wired**: choosing the tool asks for the file in the browser's chooser at once; the picture follows the cursor by its centre, snapped to the graphics grid (`GRID_GRAPHICS`), outlined; a click puts it on the sheet as one undo step ("Place Image") and selects it; the tool goes on (the next click asks for another file) until Escape, which first drops an image in the hand; a file that is no image says "Could not load image from '%s'." (a parameter `{ name, data }` is a file already chosen, for a test) | `SCH_DRAWING_TOOLS::PlaceImage` |
| A dropped image (`eeschema.EditorControl.ddAddImage`) | **wired**: a `.png`, `.jpg` or `.jpeg` dropped on the sheet (the table of `EDA_BASE_FRAME::OnDropFiles` for this editor) is read in the browser and put in the hand as Place Image's immediate mode does -- one click places it and the tool ends; the board's editor takes no image drop (its table has no PNG) | `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::DdAddImage` |
| Image Properties (E, a double click) | **wired**: the position, and in the panel the board's Reference Image Properties shares (`components/ImageEditorPanel.tsx`) a preview, the definition, the scale and Convert to Greyscale; OK checks the scale (at least 15 pixels, over 6000 asks first, in KiCad's words) and sends one `edit_graphic` that leaves the pixels alone unless they were converted (the browser converts them with `wxImage::ConvertToGreyscale`'s weights and writes the definition back in a `pHYs` chunk); Cancel changes nothing | `DIALOG_IMAGE_PROPERTIES`, `PANEL_IMAGE_EDITOR::CheckValues`, `BITMAP_BASE::ConvertToGreyscale` |
| Drawn, picked, listed | **ported**: under everything else on the sheet (`LAYER_DRAW_BITMAPS`), opaque, with a rectangle in the selection colour round it when it is selected; a click anywhere in its box picks it (`SCH_BITMAP::HitTest`: the bounding box), Graphics in the Selection Filter gates it, the box select and Zoom to Selection use its box; Properties lists `SCH_BITMAP`: Position X and Y, Locked, Scale, Width and Height (the transform offset is not: the IR keeps none), every row one `edit_graphic` or one `move`; Edit Text & Graphics Properties leaves it alone | `SCH_PAINTER::draw( const SCH_BITMAP* )`, `SCH_BITMAP_DESC` |
| Move, Rotate, Mirror, Delete, Lock | **wired** for the position: a move and a turn carry the centre, one undo step each; the pixels are not turned or mirrored (`BITMAP_BASE::Rotate` and `Mirror` need a decoder) | `SCH_BITMAP::Rotate`, `MirrorHorizontally` |
| Not done | | the pixels turned by Rotate and mirrored by Mirror; the transform origin offset; Duplicate of an image as an array; the image on a symbol or in the Symbol Editor (not an eeschema feature either) |

Tests: `crates/model/src/image_info.rs` (6: base64, the PNG and JPEG headers, a GIF and a BMP, the hash), `crates/kicad/src/sch_extras_io.rs` (the image among every graphic kind of the round trip, `saveBitmap`'s form and the old version's scale rule), `crates/ops/src/sch_edit.rs` and `sch_move_tests.rs` (add, edit with and without pixels, refusals, move and turn), `crates/cli/src/pcb_items_tests.rs` (one undo step, the state, the route, the derived sheet and its re-import; the kicad-cli plot, slow tier), `kicad-port/{refImage,schItemGeom,schItemProperties}.test.ts` (`node --test`). `e2e/pcb-items.check.js` (its schematic half, through `window.__eda`): Place Image on the tab with its file in the hand, the click on the grid, undo and redo, Escape twice, the picture painted on the canvas; Image Properties with a scale refused, applied, converted to greyscale and cancelled, the Properties panel rows, a click that picks the image, Move and Delete; a dropped PNG placed by one click, a file that is no image refused, and the board's editor ignoring the drop.

## 37. A schematic dropped on the sheet: `ddAppendFile` runs Import Sheet with the files of the drop (`sch_edit_frame.cpp` `m_acceptedExts`, `eda_base_frame.cpp::OnDropFiles`, `sch_edit_tool.cpp::DdAppendFile`, `sch_drawing_tools.cpp::ImportSheet`, `sch_edit_frame.cpp::LoadSheetFromFile`; GAPS.md items 4 and 28, package P19; 2026-10-21)

Added on `task-microwave`. KiCad's `EDA_BASE_FRAME::OnDropFiles` looks the extension of each dropped file up in the frame's `m_acceptedExts` (`kicad_sch` is `ddAppendFile`) and runs the action with the file's path; `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::DdAppendFile` runs `importSheet` with it, which, given a file, asks for none and puts the sheet (or its contents) in the hand,
and `LoadSheetFromFile` finds the sheets that file places in the folder it is in. A browser gives the page the text of each file and no folder, so the files of the drop go to the server together and it makes the folder: `POST /api/sch/dropped_sheets` (`crates/cli/src/sheet_drop_api.rs`; the page's side is `kicad-port/{sheetDrop,fileDrops}.ts`, `api/sheetDropClient.ts`, `actions/schSheetFileActions.ts` and
`components/FileDropHost.tsx`).

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| A `.kicad_sch` dropped on the schematic editor (`eeschema.EditorControl.ddAppendFile`) | **wired**: the drop host takes every `.kicad_sch` of the drop (a drop of other kinds of file still takes the first and says the rest are skipped; the board editor's table has no `kicad_sch`, so a schematic dropped on the board is ignored, as KiCad's is), reads their text in the browser (a file over 64 MB is refused first) and runs the action with them; the browser's own drop (opening the file) is prevented | `EDA_BASE_FRAME::OnDropFiles`, `DoWithAcceptedFiles` |
| The files side by side | **ported**: the files are written in a scratch folder of the server (the temporary folder: nothing of the project's changes; folders a day old are removed as a new one is made) the way their placements say: the file a sheet places is looked for by the name in its `Sheetfile`, relative to the file that places it, matching the base name of a dropped file in either case, and a path stays inside the folder (`..` above the root is the root); a cycle or a file that places itself is read once; at most 64 files and 64 MB | `SCH_EDIT_FRAME::LoadSheetFromFile` |
| Which file is imported | **ported, for one file**: the file that no other file of the drop places, whatever the order of the drop; the reply gives its path on the server, `Cmd::ImportSheet`'s `source`, so the verb, its checks, its options and its one undo step are Import Sheet's | `SCH_DRAWING_TOOLS::ImportSheet` |
| The sheets that were not dropped | **said**: each sheet a file places whose file is not among the dropped ones is named with the file that places it, in Choose Schematic's checks ("The sheet file 'X' that 'Y' places was not found: its sheet comes in empty.", from `load_sheet_file`, which says the same of a file it reads from a folder that lacks it) and in a toast ("Not in the drop: X (placed by Y). Its sheet comes in empty."); a placed sheet that was dropped is not named | `LoadSheetFromFile` (the sheet comes in with no screen) |
| Choose Schematic | **differs**: it opens on the file, with the options Import Sheet remembers (as a sheet or as its contents, the numbering of the symbols); Open arms Import Sheet's placement tool as for a file picked in it (two clicks size the sheet, Hierarchical Sheet Properties names it after the file, Import adds it as one undo step). KiCad, given a file, has no chooser and the sheet is in the hand at once | `ImportSheet`, `FILEDLG_IMPORT_SHEET_CONTENTS` |
| Files the first does not place | **said, not imported**: "Only X is imported; Y is not placed by it (drop it on its own)." (KiCad runs Import Sheet for each file in turn) | `DoWithAcceptedFiles` |
| Refusals | **ported in words**: no files, a file that has no name or no text, a name that is not `.kicad_sch` ("X is not a schematic file (.kicad_sch)"), a drop of more than 64 files or 64 MB, a file the folder cannot take | |

Tests: `crates/cli/src/sheet_drop_api.rs` (6: a file alone, the file nobody places with its sheets beside it and the missing ones named, files left out, names in either case and paths inside the folder, a cycle and a file that places itself, what is not a schematic or is too much), `crates/kicad/src/sheet_file.rs` (a test of `referenced_sheet_files` and the missing sheet named by `load_sheet_file`),
`kicad-port/{sheetDrop,fileDrops}.test.ts` (3, `node --test`), and the last scenario of `e2e/microwave.check.js` through `window.__eda`: a drop of two files on the schematic opens Choose Schematic on the one nobody places whatever the order, names the sheet that is missing in the dialog and in the toast, Open arms the tool, two clicks and Import add the sheet, one undo removes it, and a drop whose files do not place each other
says which is imported.

## 38. The SPICE simulator: Inspect > Simulator, its window, Probe and Tune, the operating point on the sheet (`eeschema/sim/simulator_frame.cpp`, `simulator_frame_ui.cpp`, `sim_tab.cpp`, `sim_plot_tab.cpp`, `sim_plot_colors.cpp`, `spice_circuit_model.cpp`, `spice_value.cpp`, `ngspice.cpp`, `toolbars_simulator_frame.cpp`, `eeschema/tools/simulator_control.cpp`, `sch_editor_control.cpp::SimProbe`, `SimTune`, `ToggleOPVoltages`, `ToggleOPCurrents`, `eeschema/dialogs/dialog_sim_command.cpp`, `dialog_user_defined_signals.cpp`, `dialog_sim_format_value.cpp`, `eeschema/widgets/tuner_slider.cpp`, `sch_edit_frame.cpp::RefreshOperatingPointDisplay`, `common/widgets/mathplot.cpp`; GAPS.md item 34, package P21; 2026-10-23)

Added on `task-simulator`. The architecture is the user's (docs/HANDOFF.md, "Next tasks", item 3): the SPICE netlist is kicad-cli's (`kicad-cli sch export netlist --format spice`, with its `--format kicadxml` for the symbols' fields and pins), made into the
netlist KiCad's simulator gives ngspice (`crates/sim/src/netlist.rs`); ngspice runs as a separate program, `ngspice -b` on a deck that sources that netlist (`crates/sim/src/deck.rs`), found as kicad-cli is found (`EDA_NGSPICE`, then `PATH`), its raw
files read back (`crates/sim/src/raw.rs`). No chart library: the plot is a canvas painted the way mathplot paints (`components/simulator/SimPlot.tsx` over `kicad-port/sim/mathPlot.ts`). The server is `crates/cli/src/sim_api.rs` (`/api/sim/circuit`, `run`,
`stop`, `workbook/parse`, `workbook/text`); the window is `components/simulator/`, its state `state/simulator.ts`, its model `kicad-port/sim/simFrame.ts`, the actions `actions/simActions.ts`.

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Inspect > Simulator (`eeschema.EditorControl.showSimulator`) | **wired**: a modeless window over the schematic editor (moved by its title, sized by its corner), titled "*name [Unsaved] — SPICE Simulator" as `UpdateTitle` titles it; it opens on the workbook the project names, else on the schematic's own analysis (a `.tran`/`.ac`/... text, `GetSchTextSimCommand`), else on no tab; a named workbook the design does not have says "Workbook file '%s' not found. Loading simulation settings from schematic." in the info bar | `SIMULATOR_FRAME::SIMULATOR_FRAME`, `SIMULATOR_FRAME_UI::InitWorkbook` |
| The window's menus and toolbar | **ported**: File (New Analysis Tab, Open/Save/Save As Workbook, the four exports, Close), View (side panel, console, Undo/Redo Last Zoom, the seven zooms, grid, legend, dotted, dark mode), Simulation (New Analysis Tab, Edit Analysis Tab, Run, Probe, Tune, User-defined Signals, Show SPICE Netlist); the toolbar of `DefaultToolbarConfig`; every entry's enable and check condition is `setupUIConditions`' (`state/simulator.ts` `simEnabled`, `simChecked`). The frame's hotkeys (Ctrl+N, Ctrl+O, Ctrl+S, R, ...) act while the window has the keyboard, as KiCad's act in its own frame; the shared zoom and grid actions act on the plot then and on the canvas otherwise | `SIMULATOR_FRAME::doReCreateMenuBar`, `SIMULATOR_TOOLBAR_SETTINGS::DefaultToolbarConfig`, `SIMULATOR_FRAME::setupUIConditions` |
| New Analysis Tab, Edit Analysis Tab (the analysis dialog) | **ported**: the SPICE Command page (the analysis type for a new tab; AC, DC with two sources and Swap, TRAN with "use initial conditions" and its max-step rule, FFT with its signals and Linearize, NOISE, SP, PZ, OP, Custom with Load Directives from Schematic; the five save/fix check boxes; the compatibility mode) and the Plot Setup page (fixed Y1/Y2/Y3 scales, grid, legend, dotted, margins); the command made and read back as `TransferDataFromWindow` and `parseCommand` do, KiCad's messages for what is wrong ("A DC source must be specified.", "Source 1 and Source 2 must be different.", "A frequency range must be specified.", "'%s' is not a valid SPICE value."); the compatibility mode and Add full path are the project's settings, saved in the design (`set_simulator`) | `DIALOG_SIM_COMMAND` |
| Run / Stop Simulation | **ported, a batch run**: kicad-cli's netlist then ngspice in batch mode, in one request; the plot shows the run when it is over (KiCad's libngspice runs in the background and the plot follows it on a 50 ms timer, `OnSimRefresh( false )`). Stop kills ngspice's process group: the run's vectors go with it (KiCad's `bg_halt` keeps them). One run at a time ("Another simulation is already running."); a run of more than ten minutes is killed (`EDA_SIM_TIMEOUT_SECS`). The first tab is offered the schematic's changed analysis ("Schematic sheet simulation command directive has changed.  Do you wish to update the Simulation Command?"); an FFT runs on the first TRAN tab's results ("You must run a TRAN simulation first; its results will be used for the fast Fourier transform.") -- here by running that transient again with the FFT's lines after it; "Simulator requires a fully annotated schematic." | `SIMULATOR_FRAME::StartSimulation`, `SIMULATOR_CONTROL::RunSimulation`, `NGSPICE::Run`, `Stop` |
| The netlist | **ported**: `.save all`, `.probe alli`, `.probe p()` of every item of two pins but XSPICE ones, the schematic's directives without its analyses when the tab has one, the tab's command, `.include` paths made absolute ("Could not find library file '%s'"); kicad-cli's own reporter lines are the "Errors during netlist generation." message. The deck sources the netlist rather than being it: ngspice 42 in batch mode cannot read `.probe p(<voltage source>)` in a deck (it takes the probe's lines for commands and the matrix is singular), and reads it sourced as libngspice reads KiCad's | `SPICE_CIRCUIT_MODEL::GetNetlist`, `NETLIST_EXPORTER_SPICE::WriteDirectives`, `writeInclude` |
| The compatibility mode | **ported**: `set ngbehavior=...` after the netlist is read, as `NGSPICE::LoadNetlist` unsets it across the parse and sets it again; esave none unless "Save all digital event data" | `NGSPICE::GetSettingCommands`, `NGSPICE::Attach` |
| The plot | **ported**: mathplot's model -- plot coordinates 0..1 per scale, `Fit` with the 3 % top and bottom gap, the X ticks (one more every 96 pixels), the Y ticks (every 32), a slave Y scale's ticks where its master's are, the log scale's decades, `LIN_SCALE` / `LOG_SCALE` labels with their SI suffix and the unit in the axis name, `TIME_SCALE` held to the command's start and end -- the axes `updateAxes` makes for each analysis (Voltage/Current/Power, Gain/Phase in dB and degrees, Noise V/√Hz and A/√Hz, the DC sweep's swept quantity), the traces two pixels wide, dotted current or phase on request, the trace colours of `SIM_PLOT_COLORS` given in turn, dark or white background; the wheel zooms by 1.1 at the pointer (Ctrl pans left and right, Shift up and down), a left drag zooms to a rectangle or moves a cursor or the legend, a middle drag pans, the right-click menu (Undo/Redo Last Zoom, Zoom In, Zoom Out, Center on Cursor, Fit on Screen); the zoom history; the limited view | `mpWindow`, `mpScaleX`, `mpScaleXLog`, `mpScaleY`, `SIM_PLOT_TAB`, `SIM_PLOT_COLORS` |
| The signals grid | **ported**: the signals of the analysis (every net's voltage, every item's currents and dissipations by the options, the noise spectra, `.save` and `.probe` directives, the user-defined signals, AC gain and phase rows), voltages first and in natural order, the filter (a regular expression, a wildcard or a substring); Plot, Color, Cursor 1, Cursor 2 and the custom cursors as radio columns; the signal's menu (Measure Min, Max, Average, RMS, Peak-to-peak, Time/Frequency of Min and Max, Integral, Perform Fourier Analysis..., Copy Signal Name, Create new cursor...) and the cursor columns' (Create new cursor..., Delete Cursor n...) | `rebuildSignalsList`, `rebuildSignalsGrid`, `onSignalsGridCellChanged`, `SIGNALS_GRID_TRICKS` |
| Cursors | **ported**: placed at 40 % and 60 % of the plot, dragged, the value interpolated on the trace, the grid's rows (1, 2, Diff, then the custom ones) with KiCad's formats and the axis's label as column titles, an x typed in (Diff moves cursor 2), Format Time/Value... per signal; the cursor drawn as KiCad draws it (the trace colour mixed with the foreground, the triangle and the number above) | `updatePlotCursors`, `CURSOR`, `onCursorsGridCellChanged`, `CURSORS_GRID_TRICKS`, `DIALOG_SIM_FORMAT_VALUE` |
| Measurements | **ported, by a run**: the grid with its one empty row, the units of each result ("dB", "A", "W", "s"/"Hz" for _AT, "V.s", "J", "·Hz" for an integral), a power's `func dev:power`, `meas <TYPE> meas_result_<row> ...` with its echo, Format Value..., Delete Measurement. KiCad measures on the loaded vectors at once; here a measurement added after a run runs the simulation again with it | `UpdateMeasurement`, `MEASUREMENTS_GRID_TRICKS` |
| User-defined Signals | **ported, by a run**: the dialog (rows with their ids, Add and Delete), `let user<id> = <expression>` with the net names quoted as `quoteNetNames` quotes them and its echo, the traces renamed or deleted with the signals; the expression's auto-completion is not (a plain text field), and the syntax help is a line, not KiCad's page | `DIALOG_USER_DEFINED_SIGNALS`, `SetUserDefinedSignals`, `applyUserDefinedSignals` |
| Operating point, noise and pole-zero results | **ported**: "Simulation results:" and a line per vector (`VectorToSignal`, the value and its unit padded as `OnSimRefresh` pads them); the integrated noise; `print all` of a PZ | `OnSimRefresh( true )` |
| View > Show OP Voltages / Show OP Currents (`eeschema.EditorControl.showOperatingPoint*`) | **wired**: an OP run's voltages on each net's longest wire, the currents on a two-pin symbol's first pin and on the pins the model names, 3 digits auto-ranged, `LAYER_OP_VOLTAGES` / `LAYER_OP_CURRENTS`, the size `getOperatingPointTextSize` gives, knocked out of the sheet; both on by default, the choice this browser's. A net drawn in several pieces on a sheet gets one label (KiCad labels each connected piece) | `RefreshOperatingPointDisplay`, `SCH_PAINTER::draw( SCH_LINE )`, `draw( SCH_PIN )`, `SCHEMATIC::GetOperatingPoint` |
| Probe (`eeschema.Simulation.probe`, Shift+P) | **wired**: a pick on the sheet until Escape; a pin adds its current (the one of the model pin it is, for a device of several), a wire its net's voltage, named as kicad-cli's netlist names the net; enabled once a run is over. KiCad shows a voltage or current probe cursor and highlights the net under it; the studio shows a prompt by the pointer | `SCH_EDITOR_CONTROL::SimProbe`, `SIMULATOR_FRAME_UI::AddTrace` |
| Tune (`eeschema.Simulation.tune`, Shift+T) | **wired**: a symbol (or one of its fields) picked gets a tuner -- half to twice its value, the slider, the value typed, E24/E48/E96/E192 rounding, Single Run and Multi Run with its steps (each step a run, the traces drawn as a sweep per run), Save (the value to the symbol's Value field, or its `Sim.Params`, one undo step) -- "The current analysis must have a plot in order to tune ...", the tuned value an `alter` before the run. Only ideal R, L, C and DC sources are tuned (a potentiometer is not) | `SCH_EDITOR_CONTROL::SimTune`, `AddTuner`, `TUNER_SLIDER`, `UpdateTunerValue`, `applyTuners`, `calculateMultiRunSteps` |
| Workbooks (Open, Save, Save As) | **ported**: the `.wbk` written byte for byte as KiCad writes it (nlohmann's sorted keys, two-space indents and doubles, `crates/sim/src/workbook.rs`), read in version 7 JSON (comments allowed) and in the text of versions 1 to 6. The workbook is the design's: Save keeps its text and the project's `workbook_filename` in the design (`Cmd::SetSimulator`, one undo step), and the derived project gets the file and `schematic.ngspice` as KiCad writes them; Save As names it and downloads it too; Open reads a file the browser picks ("Unable to load or parse file %s"). Closing a changed window asks "Save changes to workbook?" | `SaveWorkbook`, `LoadWorkbook`, `loadJsonWorkbook`, `loadLegacyWorkbook`, `SIMULATOR_CONTROL::SaveWorkbook`, `canCloseWindow` |
| Exports | **ported**: Export Current Plot as PNG (the canvas, downloaded), as CSV (`%g;` values, CRLF rows, the x axis's vector name first), to the Clipboard (image/png), to Schematic (the PNG as `(image (at 0 0) (data ...))` in 76-character lines, pasted on the sheet with the cursor) | `SIMULATOR_CONTROL::ExportPlotAsPNG`, `ExportPlotAsCSV`, `ExportPlotToClipboard`, `ExportPlotToSchematic` |
| Show SPICE Netlist | **ported**: the netlist the current tab's command makes, read only with line numbers, and what the netlister said under it | `SIMULATOR_CONTROL::ShowNetlist`, `NETLIST_VIEW_DIALOG` |
| Which symbols simulate | **kicad-cli's**: the netlist says. A symbol placed from a library keeps its library symbol's `Sim.*` fields in the derived schematic (`ConstraintModel::symbol_sim_fields`, written as hidden properties unless the symbol has its own), so `Simulation_SPICE` sources, transistors and the built-in op-amp simulate. A symbol with no model (a generic IC) is kicad-cli's to write (it writes `U1 __U1`, which ngspice refuses) | `SIM_LIB_MGR::CreateModel`, `SIM_MODEL::InferSimModel` |
| Not done | | libngspice's background run and live plot (the architecture rules them out: a batch run); the measurement and the Fourier analysis on the loaded vectors without a new run; the signal-expression auto-completion; the probe cursors and the net highlight while probing; the IBIS models' own dialogs (kicad-cli writes them); the SP analysis's S-parameter signals (`portnum`); the multi-run cursor's run label and double click |

Tests: `crates/sim` (32: SPICE values and formats, the commands, the netlist made from kicad-cli's, the items, pins and currents, the deck, ngspice's process (found, run, stopped, one at a time), binary and ASCII raw files, the workbook byte for byte and the legacy text), `crates/kicad/tests/sim_fields.rs` (3: the library's `Sim.*` fields on a placed symbol, `extends`, the project's `schematic.ngspice`), `crates/ops/src/simulator.rs` (the command, one undo step), the slow tier `EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1 cargo test -p eda-cli sim_api` (an RC filter and the op-amp amplifier simulated against the hand-worked voltages: the OP's 0.98946 V, the RC's 12 (1 - e^-1) V at 1 ms and, tuned to 2k, 12 (1 - e^-0.5) V, the MAX measurement, a user-defined signal, |H| and the phase at 1 kHz and -3 dB at fc, a noise run's two plots, a DC sweep), `kicad-port/sim/sim.test.ts` (17), and `e2e/simulator.check.js` on `e2e/simulator-board.yaml`.

### 38.1 The review (2026-10-23, `task-simulator-review`)

An adversarial review of the port against KiCad 8303b2ad's code, function by function. Fixed, each with a test that held it to the C++:
- `GetSchTextSimCommand` takes the directives `SPICE_CIRCUIT_MODEL::IsSimCommand` (`CommandToSimType != ST_UNKNOWN`) takes, not the exporter's `WriteDirectives` test: a `.sp` directive is the schematic's analysis, `.op` with lines after it is not (`crates/sim/src/command.rs`).
- An XSPICE item and a mutual inductance keep `SPICE_GENERATOR::CurrentNames`' `I(<item>)` in the signals list (`netlist.rs`); Tune's refusal names the model as `GetDeviceInfo().fieldValue`, `GetTypeInfo().fieldValue` do ("'V SIN'", from `Sim.Device` / `Sim.Type`).
- The deck unsets `ngbehavior` before it sources the netlist, as `NGSPICE::LoadNetlist` does across the parse (with "User configuration" the netlist then runs with no mode, as in KiCad) (`deck.rs`).
- `SPICE_VALUE( wxString )` is `sscanf`, which never fails: a text with no number is 0, and "'%s' is not a valid SPICE value." is never shown (`spiceValue.ts`). So `.tran 1u 1m uic` reads "uic" as an initial time of 0, a DC start of "abc" writes 0, a cursor's x typed as text goes to 0, `fourier abc` is `fourier 0`.
- The numbers are written as glibc writes them: an exact half rounds to even (`%.3f` of 0.0625 is "0.062", `%g` of 1.828125 "1.82812"), where `toFixed`/`toExponential` round it away from zero (`formatFixed`, `formatG`, mathplot's `formatFloat`, the tuner's text).
- The analysis dialog (`TransferDataFromWindow`, `parseCommand`): `.sp` always ends in "1" (`m_spDoNoise ? "1" : ""` tests a pointer); the Noise page is not validated (the points as typed, the frequencies without ',' made '.', the reference through `GetItemName`, so a net gives `v(out,)`); a DC source is named by `GetItemName`; a second DC source of increment -1 is no second source; the FFT's signals are a set, written in the list's order and only those the filter shows; a Custom tab edited shows the schematic's analyses (`loadDirectives`), not its own text.
- Run with no tab opens the dialog and runs the tab it makes (`RunSimulation`); Probe and Tune stay enabled after Stop (`onSimFinished` sets `m_simFinished`); a click on nothing ends Probe and Tune (`return false`, `CLICK_CANCEL`); a tuner change stops a run going and runs again without the schematic's prompt (`onUpdateSim`); a run tunes to the tuner's value, not its rounded text (`applyTuners`' `GetValue()`); the step count is capped at 100 (`SetRange( 2, 100 )`).
- The plot: a cursor is named after the signal it was put on (`CURSOR::GetName`: "V(a) (gain)", a user signal's expression); a multi-run trace's cursor reads its own run (`m_sweepIndex`); a cursor is grabbed by its horizontal line too (`CURSOR::Inside`); Ctrl and Shift with the wheel pan the way `PerformMouseWheelAction` pans (they were reversed); Center on Cursor sets the position alone (`SetPos`); the popup's Zoom In zooms at the last middle click (`m_mouseMClick`); a measurement's format takes its units again on every run (`UpdateMeasurement` from `OnSimRefresh`); the Format dialog's units are tested in KiCad's order ("~V.s" is in s, "~dB" keeps its '~').

Checked and kept: the netlist conversion (`GetNetlist`'s `.save all`, `.probe alli`, `.probe p()` but for 'A', the analyses left out, `.include` paths) and the run: the RC and the op-amp boards simulated through `/api/sim/run` agree with `ngspice -b` run by hand on kicad-cli's own `--format spice` netlist to 5e-12 (OP, TRAN, AC, DC, NOISE, every vector); the workbook's bytes (`SaveWorkbook`, `SaveCursorToWorkbook`'s cursor numbering, sorted keys, nlohmann's doubles) and `schematic.ngspice`'s keys (`NGSPICE_SETTINGS`: `fix_include_paths`, `meta.version` 0, `model_mode`, `workbook_filename`).

The deliberate deviations, judged: the batch run in place of libngspice's session (a measurement, a Fourier analysis or a tuner after a run is a new run) is what the architecture allows and gives the same vectors; the deck that `source`s the netlist is needed by ngspice 42's batch mode and leaves the netlist byte for byte KiCad's; the hidden `Sim.*` fields are what a symbol placed in KiCad carries (every field of its library symbol): on `examples/ldo.yaml`, `ldo_proximity_heavy.yaml` and `mixed_track_widths.yaml` (the examples that gain them: an LED, a diode) kicad-cli's ERC is the same with and without them, the nets of the `kicadsexpr` and `kicadxml` netlists are the same, and the netlists gain only the components' `Sim.*` fields, as KiCad's would; the SPICE netlist changes as it should (a `D` with a `.model`).

Left, with the reason: the S-parameter signals (`portnum` is not in the circuit the server reads); a cleared measurement row is removed at once (KiCad leaves it empty until the grid is rebuilt; the results and the workbook are the same); a trace with no data does not stretch the scales to 0 (KiCad's `SetData` of empty vectors); a probe on an SP tab gets data where KiCad's `updateTrace` asserts; the multi-run cursor's snap to the nearest run on a vertical drag; KiCad's empty-, not-a-number- and range-messages of `wxIntegerValidator` for the points (wxWidgets' text is not on the box; the port's "'%s' is not a valid number of points." stands in); `m_netnames` (the nets `quoteNetNames` quotes) is every net, where KiCad's is every net any signals list showed (it is never cleared); nlohmann's Grisu2 can, rarely, write a double with more digits than the shortest the port writes; IBIS items' currents (one per pin).

Tests: `kicad-port/sim/sim.test.ts` (19, three of them the review's), `crates/sim` (31, the review's cases added to the netlist, command and deck tests), `e2e/simulator.check.js` (Probe after a Stop).

## 39. Schematic details, part 2: a screen shown by two sheets, buses across sheets, a label's own fields (`sch_editor_control.cpp::Paste`, `updatePastedSheet`, `updatePastedSymbol`, `connection_graph.cpp`, `sch_label.cpp`, `sch_field.cpp`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr.cpp`; GAPS.md items 4, 12 and 20, package P10; 2026-10-23)

Added on `task-sch-details`.

### A screen shown by two sheets

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| Paste / Duplicate of a sheet whose file the design shows | **ported**: the new sheet symbol names the same file and shows the same screen; the screen in the clipboard is not used. A screen pasted into itself and a screen no sheet shows (a Cut) come in as a copy, as before; a screen with sheets of its own is shared too since section 42 | `SCH_EDITOR_CONTROL::Paste` (`SearchHierarchy`, `loadedScreens`, `SetScreen( existingScreen )`, `TestForRecursion`) |
| The new placement's references | **ported**: each symbol of the screen gets an instance for the new path, numbered from the reference under the copied placement by Paste's `ReannotateDuplicates` against every reference of every placement (Paste Special's Keep keeps it); a reference that is the symbol's own needs no entry. Kept in `SchematicSection::instance_overrides`; the power symbols keep theirs | `updatePastedSheet`, `updatePastedSymbol`, `SCH_REFERENCE_LIST::ReannotateDuplicates` |
| The file | **ported**: one `.kicad_sch` for the screen, each symbol with an `(instances (project (path ..) (reference ..)))` entry per placement | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR::saveSymbol` |
| The nets | **ported**: `trace_design` traces every placement, a screen shown twice twice, its pins under the references the placement gives (`CONNECTION_GRAPH::Recalculate` over the `SCH_SHEET_LIST`); a sheet pin joins the hierarchical labels of its own placement; a local label's net is one per placement, named apart by its sheet path. A PWR_FLAG names no net and joins nothing by name (its pin is a power output: `SCH_PIN::IsGlobalPower`) | `CONNECTION_GRAPH::Recalculate`, `propagateToNeighbors` |
| The parts | **ported**: a part per reference of a placement (a copy of the screen's own part), so its pins are on the net list and its footprint on the board | |
| The sheet in view | **ported**: `GET /api/schematic?sheet=` gives each symbol the `reference` the placement gives it, in its Reference field, and its pins' nets under it; the painter draws it. The verbs still name a symbol by its id | `SCH_SYMBOL::GetRef( &GetCurrentSheet() )` |
| Left | Annotate numbers the screen's own references, not the other placements'; Delete of a placement leaves its overrides in the screen (unused: the writer, the tracer and the model read only the placements there are; KiCad's `PruneOrphanedSymbolInstances` removes them) | |

Tests: `crates/engine/src/nets.rs` (`a_screen_shown_twice_is_a_net_per_placement`), `crates/cli/src/sch_hierarchy_tests.rs` (`a_copied_sheet_is_pasted_as_a_second_placement_of_its_screen`; slow: `slow_a_pasted_sheet_shares_its_screen_and_kicad_cli_makes_a_net_per_placement`: kicad-cli's netlist of the two placements is the studio's net list, its ERC finds no `duplicate_reference`, one undo takes the placement back), and the first scenario of `e2e/sch-details-2.check.js` (Duplicate of a sheet: the same file, references of its own, nets of its own, one undo).

### Buses across sheets

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| A bus and a wire | **ported**: a bus wire's points are nodes of a layer of their own: a wire or a pin that ends on a bus is not on it (`SCH_LINE` on `LAYER_BUS` / `LAYER_WIRE`); a label or sheet pin is on the bus when it lands on a bus wire, or names a bus and nothing of the wire layer is there | `CONNECTION_GRAPH::updateItemConnectivity`, `SCH_CONNECTION::ConfigureFromLabel` |
| The bus's members | **ported**: its driver's name expanded (`eda_kicad::expand_bus_members`: a vector, a group, the design's aliases); on the bus's sheet the nets of a member's name (a local or hierarchical label, a global label or a power symbol of that name) are its neighbours and one net with it, and so are the members of the other labels on the bus; a sheet pin's members count only when the pin is what names the bus (`getDefaultConnection` makes no connection of a sheet pin) | `processSubGraphs` (`m_bus_neighbors`, `add_connections_to_check`) |
| Across the hierarchy | **ported**: a bus sheet pin and the bus hierarchical label of the same name inside are one bus (a bus never joins a net there, `Type()` must match); its members are matched by index for a vector and by name for a group (then by index), so a root bus `X[0..1]` names the child's D0 net X0 | `propagateToNeighbors`, `propagate_bus_neighbors`, `matchBusMember` |
| The member's name | **ported**: the best driver of the bus names each member net (a global or power name of a neighbour still wins: `GetDriverPriority( neighbor->m_driver ) >= GLOBAL_POWER_PIN`); a bus wire carries the bus's name, and a bus no net is on takes no name from one | `propagate_bus_neighbors` |
| Left | Bus entries play no part (as in KiCad's connectivity: the wire's label puts it on a member); a bus to bus entry (`SCH_BUS_BUS_ENTRY`) is not read; the ERC checks of buses are kicad-cli's | |

Tests: `crates/engine/src/nets.rs` (`a_bus_across_sheets_joins_its_members_nets_on_both_sides`), `crates/cli/src/sch_hierarchy_tests.rs` (slow: `slow_a_bus_across_sheets_is_one_net_per_member_as_kicad_cli_makes_it`: the hierarchical label put on the child's bus joins each member's nets across the sheets in kicad-cli's netlist and in the studio's net list, for the same name on both sides and for `X[0..1]` over `D[0..1]`; one undo parts them), and the second scenario of `e2e/sch-details-2.check.js` (PA0 of mcu30's MCU and LED sheets joined through a bus `PB[0..1]`, parted by Delete of the root's bus, joined again by one undo).

### A label's own fields

| Behaviour | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| The fields of a label | **ported**: a label's own fields past a global label's Sheet References (`SchExtras::label_fields`, by label id, in order): name, text, shown, italic. The net class field is stored as `Netclass` and reads "Net Class" | `SCH_LABEL_BASE::m_fields`, `SCH_FIELD::GetCanonicalName`, `GetDefaultFieldName` |
| Label Properties' grid | **ported**: a row per field, the Sheet References row of a global label read-only ("Intersheet reference visibility is controlled globally from Schematic Setup > General > Formatting"); Add Field gives a net class unless the label has another field, then "Field", with the look of the last row, else shown and italic; OK drops an empty unnamed row and an empty net class beside another, calls an unnamed row "untitled", refuses a name used twice ("Field name '%s' already in use.", the net class excepted: a label may name several). One `edit_label` with `fields`, one undo step | `DIALOG_LABEL_PROPERTIES::OnAddField`, `TransferDataFromWindow`, `OnGridCellChanging` |
| Where they go | **ported**: `AutoplaceFields`: under the label's text (beside it for a vertical one), the first half the text's height away (and, for a global label, two margins and the pen more), the Sheet References at the far end of the flag; `GetBodyBoundingBox` of the three kinds (`global_label_rect`: `CreateGraphicShape`'s outline). **Differs**: a field moved by hand is placed there again | `SCH_LABEL_BASE::AutoplaceFields`, `SCH_GLOBALLABEL::CreateGraphicShape` |
| The file | **ported**: `(fields_autoplaced yes)` and a `(property ..)` per field (italic, justification, `(hide yes)`); every global label has its `Intersheetrefs` property; the reader takes the others back (the Sheet References are the label's own). The clipboard carries them | `saveText`, `saveField`, `parseSchText`, `parseSchField` |
| The net class | **ported**: the label's net is in each class its fields name -- the model's (the router, the board's DRC) and the schematic's project, which now lists the board's classes (`net_settings.classes`, `netclass_patterns`): kicad-cli's ERC reports a class the board does not define (`undefined_netclass`, "Netclass %s is not defined") and its netlist puts the net in the class. **Differs**: a class the board does not define is KiCad's implicit class; here it gives the net nothing | `checkNetclassDrivers`, `NET_SETTINGS::GetEffectiveNetClass`, `ERC_TESTER::TestMissingNetclasses` |
| Sheet References | **ported**: Schematic Setup > Formatting > Inter-sheet References (show, own page, Standard (1,2,3) or Abbreviated (1..3), prefix, suffix; `Cmd::SetIntersheetRefs`, the project's `schematic.drawing.intersheets_ref_*`); a global label's field reads the pages its name is on (`GetPageRefsMap`, `ResolveTextVar( "INTERSHEET_REFS" )`), shown or hidden by the setting | `SCHEMATIC::RecomputeIntersheetRefs`, `SCH_GLOBALLABEL::ResolveTextVar`, `PANEL_SETUP_FORMATTING` |
| Left | A label's field is not an item of its own (not picked, moved or edited on the canvas); the net class of a bus label is not given to the members; Sheet References' own entries in the context menu (jump to a page) | |

Tests: `crates/kicad/tests/sch_label_fields.rs` (our writer and reader; slow: kicad-cli's own rewrite keeps the fields), `crates/ops/src/sch_props_tests.rs` (`label_properties_keep_the_labels_own_fields_as_the_dialogs_ok_does`), `kicad-port/schProperties.test.ts`, `crates/cli/src/sch_hierarchy_tests.rs` (slow: `slow_a_labels_net_class_field_is_read_by_kicad_cli`, `slow_a_global_labels_sheet_references_read_as_kicad_cli_plots_them`: kicad-cli's plot shows `[1,2]`, then `[2]` and `[1]` without the own page, as the studio does), and the last two scenarios of `e2e/sch-details-2.check.js`.

### Review (2026-10-23, `task-sch-details-review`)

An adversarial pass over the three ports against KiCad 8303b2ad's code, and kicad-cli's netlists of KiCad's own QA hierarchies against the studio's tracer.

| Finding | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| A symbol moved on a screen shown twice lost its reference on the other placement (an override names its symbol by place), so that placement showed the screen's own reference twice | **fixed**: `Board::tidy_shared_screens` after every command moves the overrides with their symbols | `SCH_SYMBOL::m_instances` (the instance is the symbol's) |
| A symbol added to a screen shown twice had the same reference on both placements | **fixed**: a new symbol gets a reference of its own on each later placement, numbered against the design | `SCH_SCREEN::Append`, `updatePastedSymbol`, `ReannotateDuplicates` |
| Delete of a placement left its overrides; a sheet pasted later where it was (the same id: an id is made from the file and the place) took them back | **fixed**: dropped on Delete, on a shared paste and on a copy (`eda_model::sheet_list::prune_orphaned_overrides`) | `SCH_SCREENS::PruneOrphanedSymbolInstances` |
| Import Sheet (and a paste copy) of a tree that shows a screen twice kept one screen and gave both placements its references | **fixed**: each later placement is numbered (`Board::number_later_placements`) | `updatePastedSheet` per path, `ReannotateDuplicates` |
| KiCad 6 / early 7 files: the root's `(symbol_instances ..)` (the per-placement references and units of a shared screen) and the sheet fields "Sheet name" / "Sheet file" were not read | **fixed** (`legacy_symbol_instances`, `is_sheet_field`) | `parseSchSymbolInstances`, `UpdateSymbolInstanceData`; `parseSchField` legacy names |
| A PWR_FLAG was told by `lib_id == "power:PWR_FLAG"`: a project library's flag (`complex_hierarchy_schlib:PWR_FLAG`) joined every flagged rail by its name | **fixed**: a power symbol names its net when its library pin is a power input (`Naming::global_power`); without the library, a symbol called PWR_FLAG in any library does not. Confirmed: a PWR_FLAG never names a net (`SCH_PIN::IsGlobalPower` wants `PT_POWER_IN`; its `#FLG` reference gives `GetDriverPriority` `NONE`) | `SCH_PIN::IsGlobalPower`, `CONNECTION_GRAPH::GetDriverPriority` |
| A power symbol of a library other than `power:` was written with units named after the whole `lib_id`: kicad-cli refused the file | **fixed** (`write_power_lib_symbol`) | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR_PARSER::parseLibSymbol` (unit name prefix) |
| A group bus across sheets matched its members by full name, then by place: `pre{A B}` over `{B A}` joined B to `pre.A` | **fixed**: by local name (without the group's `prefix.`), then, for a vector on the other side, by place in the flattened group; a group member with no name match is matched to none (**differs**: KiCad falls back to its `VectorIndex`, 0 for a plain group member) | `matchBusMember` |
| A bus label's net class was not given to its members | **fixed**: the nets of each member's name on the sheet are in the class | `checkNetclassDrivers` (`processBusMember`) |
| A label's net class on a screen shown twice reached the first placement's net only | **fixed**: each placement's net, found by the wire's pins under the placement's references | `checkNetclassDrivers` (per net) |
| A KiCad 6 global label's "Intersheet References" property (and "intersheetrefs" in any case) was read as a field of its own and written back beside the Sheet References | **fixed**; only on a global label, as `parseSchField` does | `parseSchField` (`GLOBALLABEL_MANDATORY_FIELDS`, legacy name) |
| Left | (a screen shown twice inside a screen shown twice: done, section 42); (two buses joined by a member name, `${#}` per placement and `netlists/video`'s hidden power pins: section 41); Import Sheet of the QA projects themselves is not clean (a rotated symbol of a project library lands its pins off the wires in the derived file: outside these ports) | |

The decisions of the port, against KiCad:

- *A nested sheet is still pasted as a copy*: KiCad shares it too. Should match, but it needs overrides keyed by the whole path; done in section 42.
- *Annotate does not renumber other placements*: KiCad's annotates every path. After this review no duplicate is left (Annotate with and without reset, `annotate_of_a_design_with_a_shared_screen_leaves_no_duplicate`); the order of the other placements' numbers is not KiCad's. Left.
- *Deleting a placement leaves its overrides*: KiCad prunes them, and the stale ones did come back (the same id): fixed.
- *Label fields are re-autoplaced*: KiCad keeps a field moved by hand (`fields_autoplaced` is cleared). The studio cannot move a label's field, so only an imported file is concerned (its fields come back autoplaced); left.
- *An undefined class gives the net no class*: KiCad makes an implicit class with the default numbers (`getOrAddImplicitNetcless`, priority above Default). Only its name differs (a custom rule naming it, the class shown); kicad-cli's ERC and netlist read the label's field themselves. Left.
- *A bus label's class is not passed to its members*: KiCad does: fixed.
- *Every global label writes `(fields_autoplaced yes)` and an `Intersheetrefs` property*: as KiCad writes a label it placed (`SCH_GLOBALLABEL` has the field from its constructor, the drawing tool calls `AutoplaceFields( AUTOPLACE_AUTO )`, `saveText` writes the flag for a label with fields). Matches.
- *The derived project carries `net_settings.classes` / `netclass_patterns`*: where KiCad keeps them; harmless beside the label fields kicad-cli reads (the board's own patterns plus an exact pattern per labelled net). Matches.

Tests: `crates/cli/src/sch_hierarchy_tests.rs` (`the_references_of_a_shared_screen_follow_its_edits`, `a_deleted_placement_leaves_no_references_behind`, `an_imported_tree_that_shows_a_screen_twice_numbers_each_placement`, `annotate_of_a_design_with_a_shared_screen_leaves_no_duplicate`; slow: `slow_an_imported_tree_that_shows_a_screen_twice_is_a_net_per_placement_in_kicad_cli`, `slow_a_bus_labels_net_class_is_its_members`, `slow_a_shared_screens_label_class_is_in_force_on_each_placement`, `slow_kicad_qa_hierarchies_trace_as_kicad_cli_reads_them`: 18 QA projects with shared screens, buses across sheets or label fields, traced as kicad-cli reads them), `crates/engine/src/nets.rs` (`a_power_flag_of_any_library_names_no_net`, `a_group_bus_across_sheets_matches_its_members_by_local_name`), `crates/kicad/tests/sch_label_fields.rs` (`a_kicad_6_global_labels_sheet_references_is_not_a_field_of_its_own`), `crates/kicad/src/sch_import.rs` (KiCad 6's sheet field names).

## 40. Review of the hierarchy (P4) and the schematic details (P10, P10b) against KiCad's code (`sch_edit_tool.cpp`, `sch_editor_control.cpp`, `sch_drawing_tools.cpp`, `sch_screen.cpp`, `sch_symbol.cpp`, `autoplace_fields.cpp`, `annotate.cpp`, `sch_reference_list.cpp`, `sheet.cpp`, `dialogs/dialog_sheet_pin_properties.cpp`, `dialogs/dialog_symbol_properties.cpp`, `sch_io_kicad_sexpr*`; 2026-10-23, `task-p10-review`)

Sections 5, 15, 19, 21 to 27 and 29 to 32 were read function by function against KiCad 8303b2ad, the rows marked identical first; what section 39 covers (shared screens, buses across sheets, label fields) was left out. Each divergence below that is fixed has a test that failed before the fix. The practical check is new: every root of KiCad's `qa/data/eeschema` (130 projects) brought into an empty board by Import Sheet (its contents, annotations kept), written back by the studio's writer and judged by kicad-cli's ERC (violation types and counts, both sides at the default settings) and netlist (the pins of each net) against the original: 53 judged alike before the review, 87 after (`slow_kicad_qa_schematics_written_back_are_judged_as_the_originals`, `EDA_SLOW_TESTS=1`, a ratchet; `EDA_QA_SCH_ONLY=<part of a path>` runs some).

| Divergence | Status | KiCad file:function |
|---|---|---|
| A turned or mirrored symbol on a sheet this project drew: the editor (`sch_scene::Matrix`) and the painter (`transform.ts`) turn and mirror it by KiCad's `TRANSFORM`, the tracer (`eda_engine::placed`), the field placement (`symgeom`, `fields`) and the `.kicad_sch` writer (`baked_local`) turned it the other way, mirrored it about its box and ignored a top-bottom mirror: a pin the user wired was elsewhere for the studio's nets and for kicad-cli (every orientation but 0 and 180 degrees) | **fixed**: one turn and mirror everywhere, KiCad's (`symgeom::bake`, `placed::bake_offset`, `Lin::of_symbol`, `kicad_turn` folds `SYM_MIRROR_X` into a half turn and a left-right mirror); a power symbol's angle is written as read (the writer wrote `360 - rot`) and the engine's power glyphs are turned by it (`outward_rot`) | `SCH_SYMBOL::SetOrientation`, `TRANSFORM`, `GetOrientation` |
| Two symbols of one library symbol turned differently: the cache held one entry baked through the first, so kicad-cli drew the second the first one's way (`issue18346`) | **fixed**: a copy of its own in the cache, `(lib_name "<symbol>_<n>")`, shared by references turned alike | `SCH_SCREEN::Append` |
| Paste or Import Sheet of a power symbol drawn otherwise than the design's hung (every `power:<net>` name is "known", so the `_N` names never ended; `erc_multiple_pin_to_pin`) | **fixed** | `SCH_SCREEN::Append` (the cache name) |
| A placed label had no spin of its own: it was read off the wire it ended at, then again whenever the wire moved | **fixed**: RIGHT unless the dialog gives one, kept (`m_lastTextOrientation( SPIN_STYLE::RIGHT )`); the dialog's "From the wire" sends the spin it reads | `SCH_DRAWING_TOOLS::createNewLabel` |
| Mirror of a held selection turned about the cursor | **fixed**: about the half-grid point nearest the selection's centre, held or not (only Rotate reads the reference point) | `SCH_EDIT_TOOL::Mirror` |
| Auto-rotate took a line only when the point was on it | **fixed**: any line whose bounding box (grown by half its pen) holds the point, Rust and TypeScript | `SCH_SCREEN::GetLabelOrientationForPoint` (`Items().Overlapping`), `SCH_LINE::GetBoundingBox` |
| Autoplace Fields gave a shown field with no text a row of the box | **fixed** | `SCH_SYMBOL::GetFields( aVisibleOnly )`, `AUTOPLACER` |

| A graphic line of more than two points was written as one polyline, which KiCad reads as a polygon shape | **fixed**: one two-point polyline per segment | `SCH_IO_KICAD_SEXPR_PARSER` (`GetPointCount() > 2`) |
| Symbol Properties: a new field was `Field4` on a plain symbol; "A field needs a name.", "A field named ... already exists." | **fixed**: `Field5` (`GetUserFieldName( m_fields->size() )` over the five mandatory fields), "Fields must have a name.", "Field name '%s' already in use."; the Fields Table's "Field must have a name." | `DIALOG_SYMBOL_PROPERTIES::OnAddField`, `Validate`, `OnGridCellChanging`; `DIALOG_SYMBOL_FIELDS_TABLE::OnAddField` |

To change together | `DrawSheet`, `ChangeSheetFile`, `DIALOG_SHEET_PROPERTIES` |
| Page numbers by order, not "first free"; sheets written with a fixed fill colour; the sheet's vertical fields | **left** | `GetNextPageNumber`, `default_sheet_background_color`, `SCH_SHEET::AutoplaceFields` |
